# Deferred Work

Tracks findings deferred from code reviews and other quality gates. Each section is dated and references the originating workflow.

## Deferred from: code review of story 9-9-dual-nominee-bank-accounts-donor-choice (2026-07-28)

- **Decrypt-failure sentinel is hardcoded English (`NOMINEE_BANK_DECRYPT_FAILED_SENTINEL = '[unavailable — could not be shown]'`), not routed through i18n** — on a Hindi-locale device with an otherwise fully translated pay screen, a decrypt failure will surface raw English text mid-screen (`apps/api/src/modules/claims/nominee-bank-crypto.ts:17`). Deferred: matches the pre-existing `APPEAL_RATIONALE_DECRYPT_FAILED_SENTINEL` precedent this story explicitly reused (`apps/api/src/modules/claims/appeal-crypto.ts:20`) — a systemic pattern this story correctly followed, not a regression it introduced. **Re-trigger:** if/when server-generated fail-soft sentinels are given a general localization mechanism (e.g. a machine-readable reason code the client maps through its own i18n table instead of raw server text), apply it to both the appeal-crypto and nominee-bank-crypto sentinels together.

## Seams CONSUMED / re-deferred by: implementation of story 9-8-reconciliation-review-queue-ordered-by-alert-deadline-proximity (2026-07-27)

_Story 9.8 is the ADJUDICATION surface + the first CROSS-MEMBER queue read + the PRODUCER that closes four reserved seams. This records what 9.8 CONSUMED, and the small set of forward seams it deliberately RESERVES._

**CONSUMED (the reserved seams 9.8 was built to close):**
- **9.1 takeover derivation** — `computeStaffTakeover` is now consumed: `listOpenReconciliationCases` folds every reconciling pool's takeover-eligibility (heartbeat + pool-open in the SAME scan) as `takeover`-type cases. [packages/domain/src/reconciliation/reconciliation-review-read.ts]
- **9.3 `reconciliation.manual_transcription_requested`** — now a queue case type (one row per pool, latest task). [same file]
- **9.7 `reconciliation.self-verify-screenshot-uploaded`** — now a queue INPUT: merged into the member's `(pool, member)` case (screenshot attached; the detail read mints the signed URL). [same file + apps/api/src/modules/reconciliation-review/handlers.ts]
- **9.5 reversal PRODUCER** — `reconciliation.confirmation-reversed` now has its first live producer (`appendConfirmationReversed`, the review-and-reverse action) → the 9.5 reversal-consumer reads back a confirmation out; the `held` state is now reachable. [packages/domain/src/reconciliation/reconciliation-review-write.ts]
- **9.3 per-pool duplicate statement-upload re-trigger** + **9.7 duplicate self-verify-upload re-trigger** — CLOSED by the Task-3 per-logical-case dedup: statement-transcription cases key on `(poolId)` (latest task); self-verify cases merge into the member's `(poolId, memberId)` case keeping the latest object key. A duplicate raw event no longer produces a second queue row.

**RE-DEFERRED / new forward seams (gated triggers for later stories):**
- **`in_recovery` queue flag is not surfaced (v1 = false).** facilitate-recovery writes ONLY an audit line (D7 — no event) and the v1 audit sink is not queryable, so the queue's `in_recovery` UX flag defaults false. The load-bearing AC5 property (recover changes NO outcome) is unaffected. **Re-trigger:** when a queryable recovery marker exists (a queryable audit store, or Epic-10's helpdesk ticket model) — overlay `in_recovery` from it. [apps/api/src/modules/reconciliation-review/handlers.ts]
- **Member notification is log-only (D9).** `enqueueReconciliationMemberNotify` records the reject/reverse notify INTENT (NON-PII routing keys + machine `kind`) but the provider dispatch is unwired ([[project_channels_no_live_dispatch_yet]]). **Re-trigger:** when the channel providers land (the same gate as the 8.8/9.7 notify seams), wire the enqueue to the real member-notify producer + the bilingual `contribution`-namespace copy. [apps/api/src/modules/reconciliation-review/notify.ts]
- **Epic-10 helpdesk ticket UI + the ≥2-trustee cross-pool correction event.** facilitate-recovery routes to helpdesk via a RESERVED seam (no live ticket UI); the rare 2-trustee correction (Story 7.6 D-seam) is not built. **Re-trigger:** Epic 10 (helpdesk console).
- **D5 index/perf — measured-deferred.** The reconciling-stream `events_log` scan has no dedicated `(pariwar_id, event_type)` index yet; the reconciling-cycle bound keeps the working set small (a handful of live cycles/Pariwar). **Re-trigger:** if the scan is observed slow at 4L — add a targeted journaled index (do NOT pre-build a `reconciliation_cases` projection).
- **`.limit(500)` shared-budget reconciliation (history.ts / read.ts) — now that a live reversal + reject producer ships.** The 9.5-era deferrals named "when Story 9.8 ships a reversal producer" as their re-trigger; that producer now exists. Cap sizing is NOT re-tuned in 9.8 (reversal volume is expected to stay rare — the monotonic exception). **Re-trigger:** if reversal/reject volume per (member, pool) is ever observed approaching the 500 shared bound. [packages/domain/src/contribution/{history.ts,read.ts}]

## Forward seams from: implementation of story 9-7-mismatch-detection-screenshot-upload-self-verify-surface-yellow-stuck-recovery (2026-07-27)

_Not code-review findings — forward seams Story 9.7 deliberately RESERVES for later stories, recorded here (dev-story Task 7) so the consuming story has a gated trigger rather than re-deriving them._

- **Story 9.8 review-queue consumption of `reconciliation.self-verify-screenshot-uploaded`** — 9.7's screenshot upload is PURE EVIDENCE INTAKE (AC4): it appends the evidence event on the alert stream (with the object key + the mismatch reference) and stops. There is NO live consumer in 9.7 — the same reserved-seam discipline as 9.1's takeover flag and 9.3's `manual_transcription_requested`. **Re-trigger: Story 9.8** — the reconciliation review queue reads this event (ordered by alert-deadline proximity), mints a signed read URL via `SelfVerifyScreenshotStorage.signedReadUrl`, and lets a trustee confirm (`contribution.confirmed`) or reverse — the ONLY path that greens a self-verified member. [packages/domain/src/reconciliation/events.ts; packages/domain/src/reconciliation/self-verify-write.ts]
- **The `resolved` re-green depends on a producer 9.7 does not own** — the `<SelfVerifySurface>` `resolved` state derives from a LIVE `contribution.confirmed` for (member, pool), which only the Story 9.4 matcher or the Story 9.8 trustee flow emits. The surface + read ship complete; the transition into `resolved` populates with ZERO changes here the moment either producer confirms. **Re-trigger: Story 9.8** (trustee confirm) — no 9.7 change needed; this is a note that the happy-path exit is intentionally other-producer-driven. [packages/domain/src/contribution/self-verify.ts]
- **The "yellow-stuck without a red flip" (no-deposit-after-window) auto-transition is an 8.9-tail concern, deliberately NOT added here** — the LIVE matcher never emits `no_statement_entry` red (`EMITTABLE_MISMATCH_REASONS` excludes it), so a member who paid but whose deposit never lands stays YELLOW, not red. 9.7 gives that member a path anyway via the FR-32 "Trouble with UTR?" fallback (upload with `fallback:true`, no live mismatch required). The yellow-past-window → red auto-flip stays a reconciliation-TAIL (Story 8.9) decision — adding it here would reopen the tail-window decision ([[project_calendar_aware_tail_not_window_extension]]). **Re-trigger: Story 8.9** (or a dedicated tail story) — if/when a post-close "still no deposit" red verdict is wired, the `<SelfVerifySurface>` red entry already consumes it with zero changes. [apps/jobs/src/matcher/matcher-worker.ts:63-66]

## Deferred from: code review of story 9-7-mismatch-detection-screenshot-upload-self-verify-surface-yellow-stuck-recovery (2026-07-27)

- **TOCTOU: the mismatch-reason snapshot used to gate/label a self-verify upload is read once before the multipart/scan/store sequence**, so it can go stale if the Story 9.4 matcher concurrently resolves the mismatch mid-request (`apps/api/src/modules/self-verify/handlers.ts`). Deferred: low impact — AC4's evidence-inertness invariant holds regardless (proven by the domain test asserting `count(contribution.confirmed)===0` after upload), so a stale `mismatchReason` on the recorded evidence event is a labeling nuance, not a reconciliation-authority violation. **Re-trigger:** if a review-queue consumer (Story 9.8) is ever observed acting on a stale reason without re-checking live state at review time.
- **No idempotency/dedup guard against duplicate self-verify screenshot submissions** (network retry, double-tap before the button unmounts) — each duplicate mints a new object key + a new `reconciliation.self-verify-screenshot-uploaded` event (`apps/api/src/modules/self-verify/handlers.ts`). Deferred: not a correctness violation — AC4 evidence-only intake tolerates duplicates; each just becomes a separate Story 9.8 review-queue item. **Re-trigger:** if Story 9.8 review-queue volume/noise from duplicate submissions becomes an observed operational problem.
- **The self-verify domain read's `.limit(500)` could theoretically be exceeded by a member/pool with an unusually high volume of confirmed/mismatch/reversal/upload events**, causing an old verdict to fall outside the read window (`packages/domain/src/contribution/self-verify.ts`). Deferred: consistent with the same forced-pagination-gate convention already accepted in `history.ts` (see the 9-5 deferred entry below) — not a risk unique to this diff. **Re-trigger:** together with the existing `history.ts` `.limit(500)` entry, when Story 9.8 ships and event volume per member/pool is observed growing.

## Deferred from: code review of story 9-6-status-pill-5-state-design-system-component (2026-07-27)

- **No runtime guard/fallback across the `StatusPill`/presenter/i18n-lookup chain if `ContributionStatus` ever carries a value outside the 5-key union** (`packages/ui/src/status-pill/presenter.ts:7`, `apps/mobile/components/status-pill/StatusPill.tsx`). `STATUS_PILL_SPEC[status]`, `TONE_TOKENS[vm.tone]`, `ICONS[vm.iconName]`, and `t(statusPillLabelKey(...))` all assume exhaustiveness and would fail (undefined spread / invalid element type / loud-by-default i18n throw) if violated — e.g. an older mobile build receiving a newer wire enum value before an app update. Deferred: pre-existing gap, not a 9.6 regression — the deleted inline `STATUS_TONE`/template-string-key code had the identical unguarded-lookup pattern; 9.6 centralizes it across more call sites (mobile pill + PDF ink + row label) but does not introduce the risk. **Re-trigger:** if a 6th `ContributionStatus` state is ever added without a corresponding coordinated release, or if backward-compat with older mobile builds becomes a live concern.

## Deferred from: code review of story 9-5-yellow-green-pill-flip-contribution-confirmed-as-canonical-financial-truth (2026-07-27)

- **`.limit(500)` in `getMemberAttestedContribution`/`listMemberContributionHistory` now shares its budget across 3 event types (confirmed + mismatch + reversal) without re-examining cap sizing** (`packages/domain/src/contribution/history.ts:229,302,348`). Deferred: zero live impact today — the reversal producer (Story 9.8) doesn't exist yet, so no reversal rows can occupy the shared budget. **Re-trigger:** when Story 9.8 ships and reversal volume becomes non-trivial, or if the cap is ever observed truncating a member's verdict rows.
- **`listConfirmedContributorsForPool`/`listActedMemberIdsForPool` batched reads carry no `.limit()` at all** (`packages/domain/src/contribution/read.ts`). Deferred: bounded by pool roster size today, but a pool with repeated confirm/reverse/reconfirm churn across many cycles could grow the read unboundedly since it's not capped by roster size alone. **Re-trigger:** together with the item above, when Story 9.8 ships and churn is observed.
- **Collect-then-reconcile boilerplate is duplicated across 4 read functions** (`listConfirmedContributorsForPool`, `listActedMemberIdsForPool`, `getMemberAttestedContribution`, `listMemberContributionHistory` in `packages/domain/src/contribution/read.ts` and `history.ts`) — only the final `hasLiveConfirmation` boolean check is actually shared; the surrounding query/grouping logic is hand-repeated in each. Deferred: a code-quality nice-to-have, not a correctness gap — each function operates on differently-shaped source data (single-pool vs. batched-across-pools). **Re-trigger:** if a 5th consumer needs the same reversal-backing-out logic, or as part of a dedicated domain-read cleanup pass.
- ~~**`held` tone renders inconsistently across surfaces** — the same ink as `grey` in the PDF Contribution Note (`apps/api/src/modules/member-pool/note-template.ts`), a distinct blue in the mobile Yogdaan Bahi row (`apps/mobile/components/yogdaan-bahi/YogdaanBahiRow.tsx`). Deferred: both are explicitly-labeled compile-keeping stopgaps with zero live impact — no `held` row can render until Story 9.8's reversal producer ships. **Re-trigger:** at Story 9.6, which owns the polished 5-state visual/tone system and should reconcile both into one consistent treatment.~~ **CLOSED by Story 9.6** (2026-07-27): a dedicated `status-held` design token (`packages/tokens/src/tokens.ts`, subdued slate-indigo distinct from grey) + the reusable `<StatusPill>` DS component replace both stopgaps — the PDF `note-template.ts` resolves the token's literal `status-held` hex; the mobile pill maps the `held` tone to Tamagui's `$purple` scale as a documented visual approximation (no slate-indigo scale exists in Tamagui). Both surfaces are now aligned in MEANING/intent and neither is a stopgap anymore, but they are not backed by one literal color value — an artifact was produced and the original inconsistency (grey-ink / stopgap-blue) is gone; this is a genuine closure, not a deferral, with the mobile-exact-match question resolved by BigDev as "approximation accepted" (2026-07-27 code review).
- **New cross-module dependency direction: `packages/domain/src/contribution/{read,history}.ts` now import from `../reconciliation/events.ts`**, while `packages/domain/src/reconciliation/matcher-{reads,write}.ts` already import the other way from `contribution/*` — a bidirectional coupling between the two domain submodules. Deferred: explicitly directed by this story's own Dev Notes ("the reversal event type is already in `packages/domain/src/reconciliation/events.ts` — import it, do not redefine"); no build-cycle break since both live in the same package. **Re-trigger:** if a layering/clarity problem is ever observed — consider hoisting shared event-type constants to a neutral location.
- **The canonical-truth fence's single-authority scan hardcodes the exact literal substrings of `CONFIRMED_EVENT_TYPE`'s declaration line, and its `functionBody()` test helper string-slices source between markers** (`packages/domain/tests/contribution/canonical-financial-truth.test.ts`) — both are brittle to a purely cosmetic reformat unrelated to the invariant being fenced. Deferred: revert-sanity was independently reproduced during this review and currently passes cleanly. **Re-trigger:** if the fence ever fails on an unrelated formatting-only change — harden to an AST-based or marker-independent extraction.
- **`reversal-consumer.spec.ts` (the new live-DB proof) only exercises `PARIWAR_A`** — no explicit test proves a cross-tenant reversal can't walk back another Pariwar's confirmation, though the shared `pariwarId` predicate structurally covers it (the same pattern already explicitly tested for Story 8.3's confirmed-contributor read). Deferred: low-cost addition, not a live gap today given the shared predicate. **Re-trigger:** whenever this test file is next touched — add one explicit cross-tenant reversal case alongside the existing 8.3 precedent.

## Deferred from: code review of story-8-6-yogdaan-bahi-contribution-timeline-list-virtualization (2026-07-22)

- **Blanket fail-soft in `contributionHistory` masks all error types (DB outage, bugs) as an empty passbook** (`apps/api/src/modules/member-pool/handlers.ts:174-192`). - **`openScopeTx`/id-parsing errors thrown before the try block bypass the fail-soft wrapper entirely** (`apps/api/src/modules/member-pool/handlers.ts:174-179`). Deferred: the identical structural gap exists in the sibling `resolveContributorList` handler already in production; not novel to 8.6. **Re-trigger:** if a DB-connectivity flake is ever observed producing an unhandled 500 on a member-pool route — fix at the shared `openScopeTx`/handler-wrapper level, not per-handler.
- **Per-distinct-pool identity resolution in `resolveHistory` is sequential, not parallelized** — N sequential DB-round-trip-plus-decrypt operations for a member with many distinct pools (`apps/api/src/modules/member-pool/handlers.ts:548-574`). Deferred: mirrors `resolveContributorList`'s own sequential per-row decrypt loop; the story's D6 notes scope only in-request memoization, not parallelization. **Re-trigger:** if a member with a long contribution history (many distinct pools) is observed with a slow passbook load — parallelize the per-distinct-pool resolution with `Promise.all`.
- **Migration 0081's partial index covers only the attested-rows query; the verdict-lookup (confirmed/mismatch) and pool-identity/pool-count queries have no dedicated index** (`packages/domain/migrations/0081_contribution-history-member-index.sql`). Deferred: those event types are empty today since Epic 9 is unbuilt — nothing to index yet. **Re-trigger:** when Epic 9's reconciliation producer ships and the verdict-lookup query starts hitting real rows.
- **Migration 0081 uses plain `CREATE INDEX` (not `CONCURRENTLY`), which takes a lock during index build on a live `events_log` table** (`packages/domain/migrations/0081_contribution-history-member-index.sql`). Deferred: every prior migration in this repo (0072/0073/0075/0078...) uses plain `CREATE INDEX`; matches established convention, not a diff-specific regression. **Re-trigger:** as part of a dedicated migration-safety pass if `events_log` write volume ever makes a blocking index build operationally risky.
- **`listMemberContributionHistory` hard-caps at `MAX_CONTRIBUTION_HISTORY_ROWS=500` with no truncation indicator surfaced to the member, so `totalInr` could silently understate a passbook that exceeds the cap** (`packages/domain/src/contribution/history.ts`). Deferred: documented as a defense-in-depth bound against pathological data, not an expected-scale limit (500 cycles ≈ 40+ years of monthly contributions). **Re-trigger:** if a real member's contribution count ever approaches the bound, or as part of a future pagination pass on this read.

## Deferred from: code review of story-7-6-pool-bound-payment-enforcement (2026-07-19)

- **`assertUniquePoolCollectionBindings` has cycle-wide blast radius** — one pair of pools sharing a `claim_case_id` throws `WrongPoolBindingAmbiguousError` for EVERY member resolution in the cycle, not just the ambiguous pair. The Edge Case Hunter independently rated this HIGH; the prior review pass already deliberately kept it (fail-loud money-conservative posture per D5). Reaffirmed as the correct conservative posture for a money-adjacent primitive with no live consumer. **Re-trigger:** if a real Pariwar ever legitimately spawns two pools sharing a claim in one cycle, or if a live high-throughput consumer (Epic 8/9) makes cycle-wide denial operationally unacceptable — then scope the ambiguity check to the resolving member's own pool(s).
- **A pool row with ZERO persisted snapshots folds into the `{ assigned: false }` absence signal instead of failing loud** — unlike every other integrity gap in the module. A truly-assigned member whose pool's snapshot is missing (mid-spawn / partial write) reads as "not assigned". Judgment call: a snapshot-less pool may be a legitimate transient spawn state, and the assigned pool is unknowable without the snapshot regardless. No live consumer. **Re-trigger:** when Epic 8/9 build the live resolution consumers and the transient-vs-corrupt distinction becomes observable.
- **Domain↔contracts binding-DTO shape is not lockstep-pinned** — only the verdict/reason/helpdesk-action VALUE tuples are pinned; the `MemberContributionBinding`/`PoolCollectionAccount` field set + ciphertext/plaintext PII partition can drift silently between the domain interface and the Zod DTO. No established mechanism for DTO-shape pinning (interface vs Zod, camelCase vs snake_case). **Re-trigger:** when Epic 8/9 build the domain→DTO response mapping — that mapping is the natural place to add a field-level pin.
- **Binding-uniqueness invariant (AC1.3) has no DB partial-unique backstop** — app-guarded only, consistent with the deliberate D6 "no migration" decision. **Re-trigger:** when the Epic-9 contribution-record migration lands; that author owns the DB-level backstop.

## Deferred from: code review of story-6-14-r9-special-case-voting-walkthrough, Group 4 — Tests (2026-07-14)

- **The two-connection concurrency matrix (`r9-voting-concurrency.spec.ts`) covers racing finalizes, concurrent votes-by-different-panelists, open-vs-open, and forced rollback, but not every combination the story's own "18 refinements" list calls out** — specifically vote-during-finalize (a vote landing while a finalize tx holds the advisory lock) and revise-vs-finalize (a panelist revising their live vote concurrently with a finalize) have no dedicated two-connection test; they're only indirectly exercised via lock serialization in the existing tests. **Re-trigger:** if a real race in either combination is ever suspected in production, or as part of a dedicated concurrency-hardening pass on this suite.
- **The forced-rollback test (`r9-voting-concurrency.spec.ts`, test 4) only forces failure on the `claim_state_trustee_decisions` insert** — it doesn't cover a second plausible mid-finalize failure point (e.g. the `events_log` insert for `claim.r9_outcome` itself, or the session-row `outcome` update). All three inserts are in the same scope-tx, so a rollback on any one should behave identically, but this is asserted for only one of the three. **Re-trigger:** if the finalize write-path's insert ORDER ever changes, or as part of a dedicated forced-rollback hardening pass.
- **The human-actor-gate invariant script (`scripts/claim-adjudication-human-actor-invariant/check.ts`) was extended to cover the R9 routes (per the story's own Dev Notes), but no apps/api test asserts the gate itself fires on an R9 route with a non-human actor.** Coverage exists at the script/CI level, not as a runtime regression test. **Re-trigger:** if the human-actor-gate ever needs runtime (not just static-analysis) enforcement for a specific route.
- **No test asserts panel-roster immutability directly** — i.e., that no code path can add/remove a panel member after `openR9VotingSession` (the "frozen thereafter" guarantee, D-D refinement #1). The guarantee holds today only because no write-path exists that touches `panel_actor_ids` post-open; there's no explicit regression test proving that if such a path were mistakenly added, it would be caught. **Re-trigger:** if a future story adds any write path touching an existing session's `panel_actor_ids`.
- **AC11's DB-level CHECK/NOT-NULL/partial-unique constraint set (migration 0063) has no direct raw-SQL test** — the constraints are exercised only indirectly (via the domain write-paths hitting them under normal/error conditions), not via a raw `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT`/direct-SQL probe proving each CHECK independently rejects an out-of-band malformed row. Same posture as prior stories' AC11-equivalent gaps. **Re-trigger:** as part of a dedicated migration-invariant-testing pass across the claims-adjudication tables.
- **No direct test asserts cross-Pariwar scope isolation for the R9 domain read models** (`getR9VotingQueue`/`getR9Panel`/`getR9VotesByTrustee`) at the domain-test layer — isolation is covered at the RLS-policy-regression layer (`r9-voting-policy-regression.spec.ts`) and at the apps/api E2E layer (cross-Pariwar 404 test), but not as a dedicated domain-layer unit/integration test. **Re-trigger:** if a future refactor moves scope enforcement out of RLS and into the read-model query layer, where a domain-layer test would become the primary guard.
- **`votes-by-trustee`'s API-layer response binding has no test asserting the exact wire-shape mapping** (domain `R9VoteByTrusteeItem` → contract `R9VotesByTrusteeResponse` field-by-field) — only the E2E test's `clause_id` spot-check and the domain-layer transcript tests exist. A silent field-name drift between the domain read model and the handler's response-building code wouldn't be caught by either. **Re-trigger:** if this mapping is ever hand-edited without a corresponding contract-level test update.
- **`translateR9Error`'s full error-to-status-code branch table has no exhaustive test** — individual branches are exercised incidentally by whichever domain test happens to trigger that error class, but there's no single test enumerating all ~13 branches and asserting each maps to its documented status/code. A future added-then-forgotten error class (the exact Group-2 gap this story's own review already found once) could recur silently. **Re-trigger:** as part of a dedicated apps/api error-mapping test-completeness pass (would benefit multiple claim-adjudication route modules, not just R9).
- **No test for a panel member who holds `claim.r9_vote` but is scoped narrower than the full panel** (e.g. holds the permission at a different pariwar/dimension than the one the session was opened under) attempting to vote — `R9ActorNotOnPanelError`/`R9PanelMemberUnauthorizedError` are tested for "not on the roster at all" and "on the roster but permission revoked," but not this narrower-scope combination. **Re-trigger:** if Epic 3's geo-tree RBAC resolver ever changes how panel-member authorization is checked.
- **No direct unit test calls `castR9Vote` against an already-finalized session** — the finalized-rejection path is covered via the E2E happy-path test and the domain integration spec's cancel-on-finalized test, but not a dedicated `castR9Vote`-specific one. **Re-trigger:** low priority; the guard is exercised transitively today.
- **No test for the same voter revising their vote concurrently with itself** (two near-simultaneous PATCH-style re-votes from the same panelist, distinct from the covered "two different panelists voting concurrently" case) — `R9VoteRevisionConflictError` exists in the write-path but has no two-connection race test proving it fires under real concurrency rather than just sequential-call coverage. **Re-trigger:** as part of the concurrency-matrix hardening pass noted above.
- **The apps/api E2E suite has no deny-outcome finalize test** (only the approve path is driven end-to-end) and no cross-tenant WRITE-endpoint test (open/vote/finalize/cancel under a foreign Pariwar) — only the cross-Pariwar READ (`GET .../:claimCaseId`) is covered by the existing 404 isolation test. **Re-trigger:** as part of a dedicated E2E-coverage-completeness pass on this suite.
- **`packages/domain/tests/integration/claim/r9-voting.spec.ts` has grown into a large single-file suite** (one `describe` block spanning AC2 through AC8/reads) — splitting it by AC or by write-path/read-model would improve navigability and reduce blast radius when one section needs a fixture change, but is a pure organizational nicety with no correctness implication. **Re-trigger:** opportunistically, the next time this file needs substantial editing.
- **Admin UI component tests remain unwritten** — duplicate of Group 3's own deferred item (`R9CasePanel.tsx`/`R9VotingPage.tsx`/`R9VotingRoute.tsx` have zero render/interaction test coverage); recorded once, tracked from Group 3's entry above, not duplicated as a separate open item here.
- **`computeR9Outcome`'s test coverage is uneven across `N`** — N=1/3/4/5 are covered (matching the story's stated boundary set), but there's no systematic sweep proving the `approve > N/2` / `⌈2N/3⌉` / `=== N` formulas hold for arbitrary N (e.g. a property-style test). A nitpick, not a gap against any stated AC (the story explicitly scopes to N∈{1,3,4,5}). **Re-trigger:** only if a future clause introduces a materially different panel-size range.
- **The lockstep-test-fixture pattern (`R9_OUTCOME_FROM_STATES` vs `TRUSTEE_ROUTABLE_STATES`, `R9_VOTING_CLAUSE_IDS` vs the niyamavali-engine's `route_r9_voting` set, contracts vs domain wire vocabulary) is now used three separate times in this story alone, each hand-written slightly differently** — an architectural question of whether a shared `assertLockstep(a, b, label)` test helper would reduce duplication and inconsistency risk, versus keeping each lockstep test bespoke to its own domain-cycle-avoidance reason. Not a defect; a design question for whoever next adds a fourth lockstep pair. **Re-trigger:** the next story that needs a new cross-package/cross-module value-alignment test.

- **`WaConfigForm` doesn't `reset()` when its `initial` prop changes after mount.** A background refetch (e.g. another admin's concurrent edit landing while this admin's tab is open) won't update an already-rendered form — RHF's `defaultValues` only applies at mount. Low-probability, admin-only, matches the story's "no gold-plating" scope. **Re-trigger:** if multi-admin concurrent editing of WA config becomes a real workflow.
- **Migration `0038_pariwar-wa-config.sql` grants `DELETE` on both new tables to `twt_app` with no accessor/handler in this diff ever using it.** Harmless, disclosed in the migration comment as "for completeness/RTBF-adjacent cleanup." **Re-trigger:** if an unused-privilege lint/audit pass is ever run against the schema.
- **Audit write in `handlers.ts` (`putWaConfig`/`putWaTemplate`) commits durably on `deps.servicePool`, separate from the `scopeTx.tx` config/template write, with no compensating-audit mechanism if the scoped write later rolls back.** The codebase has two precedents: full P1-compensation for legal/medical consent writes (`member-terms.handlers.ts`, `medical.handlers.ts`), and simple fire-and-forget for lower-stakes admin actions (`validity_cache.invalidate_all`). This diff follows the simpler precedent, appropriate to an admin config write. **Re-trigger:** if WA config/template writes are ever treated as compliance-significant (e.g. audited for legal/regulatory purposes) rather than operational admin actions.
- **`toMsisdn` (`packages/channels/src/providers/whatsapp-business.ts:2644-2646`) only strips a leading `+`, no further E.164 format validation.** Matches the same shallow handling used elsewhere at provider seams; real validation happens upstream at ingestion. **Re-trigger:** if a malformed `target.address` is ever observed reaching a provider send.
- **Test coverage gaps:** no integration test for "PUT template before config exists" (the FK-violation path); no test for a structurally-invalid PUT body (400) despite OpenAPI documenting it; no test for `ChannelConfigPage`'s own loading/error/empty-state branches (only the child forms are unit-tested); `apps/api/tests/unit/whatsapp-composition.test.ts` types its fake `Db` as `{} as never`, suppressing compile-time checks against the real `Db` shape. **Re-trigger:** alongside any future change to the config/template write paths or the composition seam's `Db` dependency shape.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-7-lock-in-clock-widget-on-home-screen (2026-06-29)

- **D1 — `clauseId` constant not snapshot-stored in event payload.** `handlers.ts` supplies `clauseId` from `LOCK_IN_POLICY_CLAUSE_ID` (compile-time constant) rather than from the `lock_in_entered` event payload. If the policy clause is ever renamed or restructured, existing members' deep-links will point to the new clause ID paired with their old `clauseVersion` UUID. Fix requires storing `clauseId` in the `LockInEnteredPayloadSchema` alongside `lock_in_policy_version`. **Re-trigger:** if the Niyamavali clause registry ever supersedes `niy.lock-in.policy` with a new clause ID.

- **D2 — Server `setDate`/`getDate` uses local timezone for `unlockDate` computation.** `handlers.ts:71` uses `new Date().getDate()` / `setDate()` which operate in the server's local timezone. Latent off-by-one near DST transitions if the server ever moves off UTC. Currently no bug (production server is UTC). **Re-trigger:** if server deployment timezone changes or a DST-observing TZ is used.

- **D3 — No `memberExists` guard on the lock-in-status read endpoint.** A member whose event stream is absent (RTBF, test cleanup) returns `{ state: 'pending-kyc', lockIn: null }` (200 OK) rather than any error signal. Pre-existing pattern across all read endpoints (vyawastha-shulk/status, etc.); not introduced by 3.7. **Re-trigger:** if a future consumer branches on state to distinguish "not yet signed up" from "record deleted."

- **D4 — Dev Agent Record job count discrepancy.** The record claims "16 static jobs green" but enumerates 17 items; project memory records 14 `ci:local` jobs. Bookkeeping inconsistency only; actual code is unaffected. **Re-trigger:** if the ci:local job list is audited for accuracy.

- **D5 — React Query `refetchOnWindowFocus` is a no-op in React Native without AppState wiring.** `useLockInClockQuery.ts` relies on home-tab remount for refresh cadence. Spec explicitly accepts this as sufficient for day-granular countdown. If a future story requires tighter focus-based refresh, wire `focusManager` to `AppState.addEventListener`. **Re-trigger:** Epic 8 or any story requiring immediate post-background refresh.

---

## Deferred from: code review of 3-6b-vyawastha-shulk-payment-upi-reference-code-seam-lock-in-gate (2026-06-29)

- **D1 — `pgViolation` `.cause` fallback fragility in `receipt-write.ts`.** `isReceiptTrDuplicate` falls back to `err.cause` only when `e.code === undefined`. If a future Drizzle version sets its own `code` property on the wrapper error, the inner pg `23505` is never seen, causing a duplicate `tr` to propagate as an uncaught 500. Deliberately mirrors 3.6a's `isMemberIdentityDuplicate`; architectural, not 3.6b-introduced. **Re-trigger:** if Drizzle updates its error wrapper shape or if a duplicate-`tr` 500 is observed in integration testing.

- **D2 — Headline test missing `tr=` assertion in the UPI URL.** `vyawastha-shulk.spec.ts` asserts `pa=`, `am=`, and `tn=` in `upiUrl` but not `tr=`. The implementation is correct; no regression guard exists if the URL construction drops the `&tr=...` segment in a future refactor. **Re-trigger:** add the assertion alongside any refactor of the intent handler's URL builder.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-3a-digilocker-provider-interface-abstraction (2026-06-26)

- **XPath injection risk in `mapper.ts` `attr()` / `text()`.** `localName` is interpolated directly into the XPath expression (`//*[local-name(.)='${localName}']`) without escaping. Currently called only with string literals (`'Poi'`, `'Pht'`, `'UidData'`) so there is no active vulnerability. **Re-trigger:** if future eAadhaar format handling ever derives element names from input data, or if `attr()`/`text()` are reused with non-literal arguments.

- **`assertRedirectUriAllowed` in `initiate` always self-validates.** `initiate` calls `assertRedirectUriAllowed(config, config.redirectUri)` — `config.redirectUri` is always in `config.redirectUriAllowlist`, making the check vacuous today. The guard is architecturally correct for a future surface where the caller supplies a dynamic redirect URI. **Re-trigger:** Story 3.3b, when the signup route passes a caller-controlled `redirect_uri` to `initiate`.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-4-nominee-declaration-with-75-25-split (2026-06-27)

- **D1 — `split_pct` no DB CHECK constraint.** `member_nominees.split_pct` is `smallint NOT NULL` with no `CHECK (split_pct IN (25, 75, 100))`. Server derivation is the correct enforcement layer (R4), but a direct SQL insert bypassing the application could write any value. Defense-in-depth hardening only. **Re-trigger:** add a CHECK in a future migration (e.g., alongside the Story 3.12 RTBF schema work).

- **D2 — Concurrent re-declarations → PK conflict → 500.** Two simultaneous `POST /member/nominees` from the same member both DELETE then INSERT the same `(member_id, rank)` composite PK; the second INSERT gets a PK violation → unhandled 500 instead of graceful behavior. Unlikely within the 30-min signup session TTL. **Re-trigger:** when the nominee surface is exposed via Life Events (Story 3.9) where re-declarations under normal use are more frequent; add serializable isolation or an `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` strategy then.

- **D3 — `getMemberStateAt` returns `null` for non-existent member, bypasses terminal guard.** `TERMINAL_STATES.has(null)` = `false`; execution continues until the FK on `member_nominees → members` raises a 500 instead of a clean 404/409. The session guard provides the first line of defence and the FK protects data integrity. **Re-trigger:** add explicit null-state check (`if (!state) throw new NotFoundError(...)`) during any handler refactor that touches the lifecycle guard pattern.

---

## Deferred from: code review of 3-5-medical-disclosure-with-ima-list-concealment-denial-ack (Chunk B — API + Contracts layer, 2026-06-27)

- **W4 — `MemberStreamConcurrencyError` → 500 on concurrent submit.** `projectMemberState` can throw `MemberStreamConcurrencyError` (unique constraint on `(stream_id, event_version)`); this error is not caught and falls through to the generic 500 handler. The scope tx rolls back cleanly, but the client receives `internal.error` instead of a retryable conflict. Pre-existing: same gap in 3.4 (no nominee concurrency handler). **Re-trigger:** Story 3.9 Life Events, where concurrent re-disclosure is more likely in normal use.

- **W5 — `TERMINAL_STATES` local hardcoded set.** `const TERMINAL_STATES = new Set(['withdrawn', 'anonymized'])` in `medical.handlers.ts`; if the domain state machine adds a new terminal state (e.g., `'deceased'`), this check silently accepts medical disclosures for members in that state. **Re-trigger:** any state-machine extension that adds a state which should block medical disclosure.

- **W6 — TOCTOU between clause resolutions under READ COMMITTED.** Both `submit` (resolveImaList → resolveConcealmentClause) and `imaList` GET (same pair) make two separate queries; under READ COMMITTED isolation a Niyamavali amendment committing between them produces a mixed-provenance row (submit) or mismatched catalog response (imaList). Rare; amendments are admin-controlled. **Re-trigger:** if amendment frequency increases, or if Epic 4 auditability requires row-level snapshot consistency.

- **W7 — Read-only handlers open write-capable scope tx.** `status` and `imaList` do only SELECTs yet call `openScopeTx` (write connection, full transaction overhead). **Re-trigger:** performance audit or connection-pool pressure.

- **W8 — POST submit missing per-actor rate limit.** Global IP ceiling applies but does not throttle by authenticated member; a stolen JWT can drive unbounded append-only writes + audit + consent rows. **Re-trigger:** when per-actor rate limiting is added to other member-write routes.

- **W9 — Encryption context doesn't include field name.** `encContext` uses identical `{ pariwarId, fieldClass: 'member_medical' }` for both `disclosedConditionsCiphertext` and `additionalContextCiphertext`; under AEAD, swapping the two ciphertexts on the same row passes authentication. **Re-trigger:** codebase-wide encryption context convention review.

- **W10 — `emitAuthAudit` no rejection capture.** All `emitAuthAudit` callers are fire-and-forget with no `.catch()`; rejection on pool exhaustion or network partition is silently swallowed. Pre-existing codebase-wide pattern. **Re-trigger:** when a `.catch(logger.error)` convention is adopted globally.

- **W11 — `status` returns 200 for non-existent member.** `{ latest: null, historyCount: 0 }` is indistinguishable from "member exists, nothing disclosed yet". The session guard is the primary defence. **Re-trigger:** if the session guard is ever loosened or ghost-member scenarios require explicit detection.

- **W12 — `unknownCodes` in error message.** `unknownCodes.join(', ')` in the `BadRequestError` message — submitted IMA condition codes (potentially PHI) appear in structured logs and the API error response. **Re-trigger:** PHI-scrubbing logging convention adoption.

- **W13 — `ServiceUnavailableError` default code `'request.unavailable'`.** Default code is semantically incorrect for a 503; all callers pass explicit codes so the default is never reached in production. **Re-trigger:** if a caller omits the explicit code argument.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-5-medical-disclosure-with-ima-list-concealment-denial-ack (Chunk C — Tests + Mobile + i18n, 2026-06-27)

- **W14 — `toSummary` ZodError on bad `acknowledgment_text_locale` in a historical row.** `MedicalAckLocale.parse(row.acknowledgmentTextLocale)` throws if a row has a locale outside `['en','hi']` (the column is plain `text`, no DB CHECK constraint). Any `GET /status` or re-submit `buildStatus` for that member returns 500. **Re-trigger:** add `CHECK (acknowledgment_text_locale IN ('en','hi'))` in a future migration — good candidate for the Story 3.12 RTBF schema work.

- **W15 — `conditionCodes.max(50)` blocks submit when IMA list exceeds 50 entries.** `MedicalDiscloseRequest.conditionCodes` is capped at 50 items; the `ImaListPayloadSchema` payload has no upper bound on the conditions array. A member wanting to submit all listed conditions would receive a 400 if the catalog ever grows beyond 50. **Re-trigger:** if the `niy.medical.ima-list` clause is updated with >50 conditions — raise the contract cap to match.

ADR-0022 is the architecture-mandated "cache-safety committed in an ADR" artifact. Per [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]], every deferral below carries an explicit re-trigger.

**Closed by [edit] (Story 2.5):**

Build green (`js_bundle_bytes: 0`, `page_weight_bytes: 4025`); live server verified (200, `Cache-Control` + `Vary`, zero `Set-Cookie`). See ADR-0022.
- **CR-D1-2.1 / Story 2.1 `@twt/i18n/react` subpath split** (Story 2.1 deferral, re-trigger = first non-React server consumer) — **Closed by [edit].** `apps/public` Astro SSR is that consumer. The React hooks (`LocaleProvider`/`useLocale`/`useT`) moved to `packages/i18n/src/react.ts` (the `@twt/i18n/react` export); the package root + `locale.ts`/`resolver.ts` are now React-free (server-safe). `apps/admin`/`apps/mobile` re-tested (no break — neither imported `@twt/i18n` yet). (`packages/i18n/src/{locale,react,resolver,index}.ts`, `package.json` `exports`.)
- **D13-1.2 / D8-1.5 (live-render scrape leg): the architecture-committed `public-pages/scrape-test.spec.ts`** (Story 1.16b deferral, re-trigger Story 2.5/11a.2) — **Closed by [edit].** Realized at `apps/public/tests/integration/public-pages/scrape-test.spec.ts` (inside the `@twt/public` workspace so it resolves `@twt/*` + transitive deps cleanly and runs in the existing `test` CI job every PR; root `tests/integration/README.md` points here). It feeds the pure `@twt/contracts` engine the REAL Niyamavali render HTML from fixtures, asserts `detectNakedPii` finds nothing + `evaluateSnapshot` passes, with a NEGATIVE CONTROL proving teeth. gate-inventory `pii-scrape` → live-render ENFORCING. **The tier-leak leg stays deferred** (matrix is empty — below).

Supersedes the pragmatic `apps/api/.../render-diff.ts` placeholder.

- **CR-D0-1.16b (non-public `RenderSnapshot` `fields` caveat / L1334)** — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** The 2.5 scrape spec constructs ONLY a `public` snapshot (where `detectNakedPii` is active), so the "non-public render with no `fields` silently passes" caveat is not exercised. **Re-trigger:** Epic 11a/11b authenticated-member/operator render snapshots — ensure they always carry `fields`.

- **Authenticated-fragment registry (NEW — Story 2.5)** — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** `apps/public/COMPOSITION-CONTRACT.md` initialises the fragment registry EMPTY; Story 2.5 ships zero authenticated fragments, so no `apps/api/src/modules/public-pages/` HTTP module is created. **Re-trigger:** Epic 11a (Member Directory) + Epic 11b (per-claim + In Memoriam) populate the registry + stand up the authenticated-fragment API.
- **Data-path alternative — thin public read endpoint in `apps/api`** (NEW — Story 2.5; the one open architectural question) — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** Story 2.5 reads public-tier Niyamavali directly from `@twt/domain` under `withPublicScope` (the public renderer holds DB creds; acceptable under `twt_app` + RLS + public-tier-only + the scrape gate). The defensible alternative (a thin `apps/api` public read endpoint keeping DB creds out of the renderer) is the recorded open question. **Re-trigger:** architect confirmation, or Epic 11b when `apps/api/src/modules/public-pages/` lands and a consolidation is natural.

## Story 2.4 — resolutions + new deferrals (Niyamavali amendment workflow admin UI + audit-logged publish, 2026-06-21)

Closure-language posture per [[feedback_closure_language_precision]]: **Closed by [edit]** only where this story produced the artifact; everything else is **Resolved via explicit deferral / Carry forward** (gap intentional, rationale + re-trigger recorded). Story 2.4 turns the Story 2.3 `[PRIMITIVE]` into a trustee `[SURFACE]`: the server-persisted `clause_drafts` draft store (the central net-new design — ADR-0021), the `apps/api/src/modules/rules/` HTTP surface, the `apps/admin/src/modules/niyamavali-admin/` React surface, and the audit-or-throw publish path. It is the FIRST CONSUMER of the Story 2.2 tone-review gate and the OWNER of the deferred 409/404 mapping, the audit-or-throw `audit_id` NOT-NULL contract, the `clause_versions` immutability trigger, and the amendment-ledger hardening. Per [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]], every new deferral below carries an explicit re-trigger.

**Closed by [edit] (Story 2.4):**

- **Story 2.4 binding deliverables (AC1–AC8)** — **Closed by [edit].** Landed: branded `ClauseDraftId`; the `clause_drafts` schema + tenant-isolation RLS (`packages/domain/src/{schema/clause_drafts.ts,policies/clause-drafts-rls.ts}`); migration `0015` (CREATE TYPEs + table + ENABLE/FORCE RLS + GRANTs + the partial-unique open-draft index + the AC7/AC8 triggers/index, hand-supplemented); the draft accessors + content-bound `resolveDraftSignoff` (`niyamavali/drafts.ts`) + `listClauses`/`listAmendments` (`niyamavali/read.ts`); the endpoint DTOs with `.openapi()` (`packages/contracts/src/rules/clause.ts` — `openapi/v1.yaml` updated, determinism green); the `apps/api/src/modules/rules/` routes (the scoped chain + the tone-review-gated, audit-or-throw publish); the error-mapping 409/404 (AC6); the admin `niyamavali-admin/` module + `/p/$pariwarId/niyamavali` route + client + hooks; the member-notification hook seam (AC3); unit + live-DB + API + admin test suites; ADR-0021 (drafted). See ADR-0021.
- **Sign-off PERSISTENCE — the durable which-artifact-was-reviewed record** (Story 2.2 deferral, "Resolved via explicit deferral", re-trigger Story 2.4) — **Closed by [edit].** `clause_drafts.tone_review_{by,at,content_hash}` is the durable record; `resolveDraftSignoff` (content-bound: returns a sign-off only when `signed_off` AND the stored hash matches `sha256(canonicalJson(current payload))`) is the resolver the gate consumes.
- **Gate CONSUMER WIRING — mounting `requireToneReviewSignoff` on a real publish route** (Story 2.2 deferral, re-trigger Story 2.4) — **Closed by [edit].** Mounted on the Niyamavali publish `preHandler` chain (`apps/api/src/modules/rules/index.ts`); gate-inventory flipped to ENFORCING.
- **Column-restricted immutability trigger on `clause_versions`** (Story 2.3 deferral, re-trigger Story 2.4) — **Closed by [edit].** Migration `0015` installs a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger rejecting any UPDATE of `payload`/`clause_id`/`version` while allowing `superseded_by_version`/`deprecated_at`/`audit_id` (AC7). Live-DB test proves both arms.
- **`resolveByClauseId` `asOf` default-to-DB-`now()` call-site discipline** (Story 2.3 deferral, re-trigger Story 2.4) — **Closed by [edit].** The publish + diff routes call `resolveByClauseId(tx, pariwarId, clauseId)` with NO app-server `now()` — the resolver defaults `asOf` to DB `now()` (§1.11). (AC verified by inspection; the route never injects a timestamp.)
- **De2 — `niyamavali_amendments.pariwar_id` cross-tenant guard** (2.3 code-review deferral, re-trigger Story 2.4) — **Closed by [edit].** Migration `0015` installs a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger rejecting an amendment whose `pariwar_id` ≠ the `pariwar_id` of its FK'd from/to `clause_versions` (AC8). Live-DB test proves cross-tenant reject + same-tenant allow + non-existent-ref → FK 23503 (preserving the 2.3 FK-integrity contract).
- **De4 — `(pariwar_id, created_at)` index on `niyamavali_amendments`** (2.3 code-review deferral, re-trigger Story 2.4) — **Closed by [edit].** Index `niyamavali_amendments_pariwar_created_at_idx` added in `0015`; the time-ordered `listAmendments` read path + `GET /amendments` route now exist (AC8).

**Resolved via explicit deferral / Carry forward (Story 2.4):**

- **split / merge / deprecate UI not surfaced** (NEW — Story 2.4) — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** AC1 lists only create / edit / preview / submit / publish, so the 2.4 admin UI surfaces create + amend only. The domain ops `splitClause`/`mergeClauses`/`deprecateClause` (Story 2.3 `write.ts`) remain available + unchanged but are NOT exposed in the trustee UI. **Re-trigger:** a later surface story that needs registry restructuring (lineage editing) in the admin UI.
- **Rendered-content-diff depth + guided payload authoring** (NEW — Story 2.4) — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** The diff-preview renders the display-shaped fields present in the opaque payload (a readable key:value before/after fallback); the authoring form captures guided display fields (`rule_code`, `title_en`, optional `title_hi`) + the workflow metadata, NOT rule-specific scalar fields (the payload is opaque — freeze row 14; Epic 4 owns rule semantics) and NOT a raw-JSON editor (Open Decision #4). The authoritative bilingual display contract crystallizes at **Story 2.5**'s public render. **Re-trigger:** Story 2.5 (display contract) + Epic 4 (rule-specific field authoring).
- **De2 trigger is `SECURITY INVOKER` — a residual under-RLS raw-SQL gap** (NEW — Story 2.4) — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** The De2 `BEFORE INSERT` guard reads `clause_versions` under the caller's role; it catches a cross-tenant attempt observed from an RLS-bypassing context (the legitimate scoped write path can never create one — `amendClause` resolves from/to within scope, and `withCheck` pins `pariwar_id`), and skips the not-visible (NULL) case so a non-existent ref falls through to the FK (preserving the 2.3 23505/23503 contracts). A fully RLS-independent hardening is a **composite FK** `(clause_version_id, pariwar_id)`. **Re-trigger:** if a defense-in-depth audit wants the composite-FK guarantee, or `SECURITY DEFINER` owned by a BYPASSRLS role is introduced.
- **Orphan audit line on a post-audit publish rollback** (NEW — Story 2.4) — **Resolved via explicit deferral (accepted edge).** The publish writes the audit line FIRST (own connection, own tx — the amendment ledger is append-only so `audit_id` cannot be back-filled) then mints the version on the scope tx. If a later step fails and the scope tx rolls back, the audit row survives as an append-only record of an *attempted* publish whose `resourceLocator` references a now-absent `clause_version_id`. This is acceptable for an append-only audit log (it records intent) and preserves the only hard invariant — no published clause without `audit_id`. Documented in ADR-0021. **Re-trigger:** none planned (intentional).
- **Story 0.13 counsel-return dependency** — **Carry forward (non-gating).** This Niyamavali amendment surface is in the Story 0.13 counsel-return integration list, but **0.13 does NOT gate Epic 2 closure** (seeded/authored content is provisional). Do not block on legal review. [Source: deferred-work.md L797/L809; epics.md L1399.]

## Deferred from: code review of 2-3-niyamavali-rule-registry-data-model-amendment-with-diff-storage (2026-06-21)

- **De1 — `lineageForward`/`lineageBackward` two sequential queries without transactional isolation** (`packages/domain/src/niyamavali/read.ts:103-154`): each function makes two back-to-back reads (all version ids, then the overlapping/referenced rows) with no REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE guarantee. A racing amendment between the two reads can produce a stale lineage result. Not a correctness issue at current scale; post-Epic-2 cleanup (add a `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ` wrapper or merge to a single CTE). Re-trigger: end of Epic 2 or first evidence of stale lineage in production.
- **De2 — `niyamavali_amendments.pariwar_id` not cross-validated against FK'd clause versions' pariwar_id** (`niyamavali_amendments.ts` + migration 0014): the amendment's `pariwar_id` column is guarded by the RLS `withCheck` but no DB CHECK constraint or trigger verifies that `from_clause_version_id` / `to_clause_version_id` belong to the same `pariwar_id`. A bug in accessor code (or raw SQL) could insert a cross-tenant amendment row that RLS would still allow (the amendment row's pariwar_id matches). Add a CHECK or a BEFORE INSERT trigger in a forward migration. Re-trigger: Story 2.4 when the audited write path is established.
- **De3 — `isUniqueViolation` fragile against future Drizzle error-wrapping changes** (`packages/domain/src/niyamavali/write.ts:50-53`): the function inspects `err.code` and `err.cause?.code` for `'23505'`. If a future Drizzle upgrade changes wrapping depth or property names, unique-violation detection silently falls through and callers get a raw DB error instead of typed `ClauseIdConflictError`. The race path (the pre-check passes, then the DB unique index fires) is not tested. Re-trigger: Drizzle major version upgrade or a CI failure where a 23505 escapes as untyped.
- **De4 — No `(pariwar_id, created_at)` index on `niyamavali_amendments`** (migration 0014): the amendment ledger's two current indexes are query-path-specific (`pariwar_from_idx`, `pariwar_to_idx`). A "list all amendments for this Pariwar ordered by creation time" query (the most natural audit/UI consumer) will do a filtered sequential scan. Add `INDEX niyamavali_amendments_pariwar_created_at_idx ON niyamavali_amendments (pariwar_id, created_at)` in a new migration once the Story 2.4/2.5 list-amendments route is built. Re-trigger: Story 2.4/2.5 introduces that route.
- **De5 — `lineageForward`/`lineageBackward` unbounded array parameters** (`packages/domain/src/niyamavali/read.ts:109, 141`): `lineageForward` passes all of a clause's `clauseVersionId`s to `arrayOverlaps`; `lineageBackward` passes all predecessor version ids to `inArray`. For a clause with hundreds/thousands of versions or predecessors, these become very large Postgres bind-parameter lists. Add chunking (split into batches of ≤500) or restructure to a single CTE once version counts grow. Re-trigger: end of Epic 2 or when a clause exceeds ~100 versions in staging.
- **De6 — `ClauseResolutionQuery` z.union XOR future fragility** (`packages/contracts/src/rules/clause.ts:135`): the XOR exclusion relies on `z.union` trying each `.strict()` member in order — an object with both `clauseId` and `clauseVersionId` is rejected because the first branch's `.strict()` rejects `clauseVersionId` as an unknown key, and the second branch's `.strict()` rejects `clauseId`. This is correct but depends on `z.union` + `.strict()` semantics; a shape change to either branch could silently break the XOR. Refine to `z.discriminatedUnion('clauseId', ...)` (once Zod supports discriminators on optional/presence) or add a `.refine()` cross-key check. Re-trigger: any shape change to `ClauseResolutionQuery` members.

## Story 2.3 — resolutions + new deferrals (Niyamavali rule registry data model + amendment-with-diff author-commit, 2026-06-21)

Closure-language posture per [[feedback_closure_language_precision]]: **Closed by [edit]** only where this story produced the artifact; everything else is **Resolved via explicit deferral / Carry forward** (gap intentional, rationale + re-trigger recorded). Story 2.3 lands the registry **shape** — `clause_versions` + the append-only `niyamavali_amendments` diff ledger + tenant-isolation RLS + the domain accessors + the transport contracts + the structural `pool`-tagged seed — and flips the benefit-mechanism gate's checks (a) + (c) from no-op to ENFORCING (AC8). Per [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]], every deferral below carries an explicit re-trigger so the commitment cannot decay un-gated.

**Closed by [edit] (Story 2.3):**

- **Story 2.3 binding deliverables (AC1–AC8)** — **Closed by [edit].** Landed: branded `ClauseVersionId` + the first non-UUID `ClauseId` (`CLAUSE_ID_REGEX` + `clauseId`/`InvalidClauseIdError`, `packages/domain/src/ids/`); the `clause_versions` + `niyamavali_amendments` Drizzle schemas (+ the `benefit_mechanism` pgEnum); the two tenant-isolation RLS policy pairs (`packages/domain/src/policies/{clause-versions,niyamavali-amendments}-rls.ts`); migration `0014` (CREATE TYPE + 2 tables + ENABLE/FORCE RLS + GRANTs + the amendments append-only triggers, hand-supplemented); the `niyamavali/` accessor module (`createClause`/`amendClause`/`splitClause`/`mergeClauses`/`deprecateClause` + `resolveByClauseId`/`resolveByClauseVersionId` + `lineageForward`/`lineageBackward` + `computePayloadDiff` + `assertAffectedMemberScope` + `ClauseIdConflictError`/`ClauseNotFoundError`); the transport contracts (`packages/contracts/src/rules/clause.ts` — `ClauseIdSchema`/`ClauseVersionResponse`/`NiyamavaliAmendmentResponse`/`AffectedMemberScopeSchema`/`ClauseResolutionQuery`, plain `z.*`, `openapi/v1.yaml` byte-identical) + the benefit-mechanism anti-drift + type-assignability tests; the `.sql` seed; unit + live-DB integration suites; ADR-0020 (drafted); gate-inventory + adr-index updates. AC8 confirmed: `pnpm benefit:check` green with 3 real `pool` records (checks a + c ENFORCING). See ADR-0020.
- **TS / object-literal rule-record seed extraction** (re-triggered from Story 1.16d deferred-work, "Resolved via explicit deferral (Story 1.16d)" bullet) — **Closed by [edit].** The Story 2.3 seed landed as `.sql` (`packages/domain/seed/niyamavali-v1-clauses.sql`), wired via `rule_sources.seed_globs`; the existing `extractFromSqlInserts` extractor covers it (3 records extracted + validated). A TS-literal extractor is **not needed at 2.3** — closed, not carried.

**Resolved via explicit deferral / Carry forward (Story 2.3):**

- **`collectMigrationSql` scans ALL migration files** (re-triggered from the 1.16d code-review deferral, "collectMigrationSql scans ALL migration files on every CI run") — **Carry forward.** The `clause_versions` migration now exists, so the re-trigger fired, but the fix is a performance optimization (not correctness): filter `collectMigrationSql` to files that mention a configured table name. **Re-trigger:** end of Epic 2 OR when the CI scan time regresses (post-Epic-2 O(N) scan over hundreds of migrations). Not a correctness issue at 2.3 (15 migrations).
- **`extractFromJsonSeed` may extract sibling non-rule objects** (re-triggered from the 1.16d code-review deferral) — **Carry forward.** The 2.3 seed is `.sql`, not `.json`, so the JSON-extractor path is **not exercised** at 2.3 (the scoping defect cannot fire). **Re-trigger:** the first story that lands a multi-type `.json` rule seed — scope `extractFromJsonSeed` to the declared table's records then.
- **SQL `NULL` literal produces a misleading finding message** (re-triggered from the 1.16d code-review deferral) — **Carry forward.** Confirmed: the 2.3 seed uses explicit `'pool'` literals for every `benefit_mechanism` value — no `NULL` is present, so this path is **not triggered**. **Re-trigger:** when a `NULL` `benefit_mechanism` is first encountered in a migration/seed — improve the finding message ("missing required tag" vs "not one of pool|reserve").
- **Column-restricted immutability trigger on `clause_versions`** (NEW — Story 2.3) — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** `clause_versions` is NOT fully append-only (`superseded_by_version` + `deprecated_at` are legitimately mutable), so a block-all-UPDATE trigger (the events_log posture) is wrong for it. Historical immutability of `payload`/`clause_id`/`version` is enforced at the DOMAIN layer at 2.3 (the accessors never UPDATE those columns). A column-restricted Postgres trigger guarding exactly those three columns against UPDATE is deferred to **Story 2.4**, when the audited write path is established + exercised under live conditions. **Re-trigger:** Story 2.4 task-list item.
- **`resolveByClauseId` `asOf` default-to-DB-`now()` at the domain layer** (NEW — Story 2.3) — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** The resolver defaults `asOf` to Postgres `now()` (DB-authoritative time §1.11; no app-server clock leaks into the domain layer). Any app-server `now()` leakage at the API call site is a **Story 2.4** concern — the first route that calls the resolver owns passing (or not passing) an instant. **Re-trigger:** Story 2.4 writes the resolver call site (ensure the route does not inject an app-server timestamp where DB `now()` is intended).

## Story 2.2 — resolutions + new deferrals (tone-guide + vocabulary enforcement process author-commit, 2026-06-20)

Scope was LOCKED at create-story: 2.2 ships the gate **mechanism**, not the persistence (no member-visible-copy publish endpoint exists yet — the first, Niyamavali publish, is Story 2.4). Per [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]], every deferral below carries an explicit re-trigger so the commitment cannot decay un-gated.

**No inherited item to Close:** a grep of this file for `tone-review` / `tone_review` / FR-69 routing returns nothing — Story 2.2 inherits no binding deferred-work entry. It only *adds* new deferrals (and Closes its own binding deliverables). The `microcopy` member-register / `copy_globs` deferrals from Story 1.17 + 2.1 are NOT fired here (this story does not widen the lint — the tone-guide doc lives in `docs/`, outside the bounded `apps/admin` scan scope).

**Closed by [edit] (Story 2.2):**

- **Story 2.2 binding deliverables (AC1–AC6)** — **Closed by [edit].** Landed: `docs/tone-guide.md` (the four named dimensions — voice; per-surface register mapped to architecture §4.15 actor-class register; prohibited frames; grief-context modulation — sourced + inline-cited from the UX spec + architecture, with the explicit "Automated floor vs. human check" §5); `docs/tone-review-checklist.md` (one item per AC1 dimension + grief-context gate + non-author-reviewer attestation + the publish-routing process); the pure domain gate (`packages/domain/src/tone-review/{errors,gate,index}.ts` — `ToneReviewRequiredError` + `toErrorResponse` 409 projector + `evaluateToneReviewGate` fail-closed evaluator; wired into `packages/domain/src/index.ts` as the `toneReview` namespace + top-level `ToneReviewRequiredError`; 5 vitest cases); the API adapter (`apps/api/src/modules/tone-review/index.ts` — `requireToneReviewSignoff` pre-handler + the dedicated `ToneReviewAuditSink` seam + `toneReviewEventToAuditInput` mapper + `recordToneReviewSignoff` helper; seam wired into `AppDeps`/`createDeps`/test-deps mirroring `auditSink`); the error-mapping 409 branch (`apps/api/src/middleware/error-mapping/index.ts`); and the teeth suite (`apps/api/tests/unit/tone-review.test.ts` — 11 cases: block-without-signoff + author==reviewer + valid-non-author + async resolver + loud-500 + never-throw + audit-input mapping + 409 mapping). ADR-0019 authored (drafted). gate-inventory Category B row added. See ADR-0019.

**Resolved via explicit deferral (Story 2.2):**

- **Sign-off PERSISTENCE — the durable which-artifact-was-reviewed record** — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** There is no member-visible-copy publish endpoint at Story 2.2, and the reviewed-artifact identity is polymorphic across surfaces that do not exist yet (a `clause_version_id` from 2.3, a future `news_post_id`, a `push_template_id`…). Manufacturing a generic `copy_review_signoffs` table now would violate the "no speculative store" discipline. The gate evaluator operates on an **injected** sign-off record; the pre-handler resolves it via `opts.resolveSignoff`. **Re-trigger:** **Story 2.4** Niyamavali publish — which already commits to recording tone-reviewer attribution + `clause_version_id` (epics.md L1485) — owns the first concrete persistence + the resolver implementation.
- **Gate CONSUMER WIRING — mounting `requireToneReviewSignoff` on a real publish route** — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** The pre-handler is installed + unit-tested but mounted by no route (gate-inventory Category B). **Re-trigger:** **Story 2.4** adds `requireToneReviewSignoff` to the Niyamavali publish `preHandler` chain (the established land-once-primitive → consumer-wires-in-with-teeth pattern).
- **Future governed copy surfaces — News/Blog · T&C · push-notification templates · helpdesk macros** — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** The tone-review checklist names these as governed surfaces, but their publish endpoints + resource-specific review permissions do not exist yet (only `niyamavali.review` is seeded). Each surface uses its own review key, added by the story that ships it — no generic `copy.review` key is manufactured. **Re-trigger (per surface, at its owning story):** News/Blog → its Epic 2/later story; T&C → Story 2.6 (T&C version pinning); push templates → their notification story; helpdesk macros → the Epic 10 helpdesk story. Each mounts `requireToneReviewSignoff` with its own resolver + review-permission enforcement.

## Story 2.1 — resolutions + new deferrals (i18n centralized utility + bilingual surface contract author-commit, 2026-06-20)

Closure-language posture per [[feedback_closure_language_precision]]: **Closed by [edit]** only where this story produced the artifact; everything else is **Resolved via explicit deferral** (gap intentional, rationale + re-trigger recorded). Story 2.1 lands `packages/i18n` — the centralized i18n utility enforcing the architectural-freeze row-10 bilingual contract ONCE at the utility level (member-visible surfaces Hindi-primary; admin surfaces English-primary; member strings carry Hindi parity verified by a build-time CI gate). Delivered: the `Locale` primitive (defined locally; value-aligned-not-symbol-identical with the domain `localeEnum`, PariwarId-at-1.7 precedent) + `getLocale(ctx)` precedence resolver (framework-agnostic — imports no HTTP type) + the React `LocaleProvider`/`useLocale`/`useT` toggle context (`react` an OPTIONAL peer dep, not a module-level singleton); the `t(key, params, options)` resolver with `{varName}` single-brace interpolation + loud-by-default missing-key/namespace/param throws; per-domain `locales/{hi,en}/{domain}.json` storage + a static-import catalog; the surface-classification registry (`locales/classification.json`, declarative-by-namespace not app-path, member-facing default); the build-time parity gate (pure `scripts/lib.ts` + impure `scripts/check-parity.ts`, naming file+key on failure, teeth proven end-to-end) wired as the `i18n:check-parity` turbo task → `i18n-parity` ci.yml job → `scripts/ci-local.sh` registration; and the AC6 numeral/currency utilities. Scope was LOCKED at create-story: AC1–AC5 (binding epics ACs) + AC6 (the deferred-work L22 re-trigger); the broader §4328 module set is deferred (below).

**Inherited item Closed (Story 2.1):**

- **`packages/i18n` numeral utilities (`toHindiNumeral` / `toGregorianNumeral` / `formatCurrency`) — the Story 1.17 L22 deferral** — **Closed by [edit].** Story 2.1 IS the named re-trigger ("Story 2.1 lands `packages/i18n`"). The three functions landed in `packages/i18n/src/number.ts` + `src/currency.ts` (Devanagari↔Latin conversion; `₹` with Indian lakh/crore grouping), with the amendment-A2 operational-vs-ceremonial split encoded as the documented contract (operational data → Latin even on memorial pages; ceremonial Devanagari prose → Hindi numerals permitted; never mixed at one hierarchy level) in code comments + README + tests. The 1.17 microcopy gate's "inline numeral formatting outside the utility" note is now anchored: `packages/i18n` is the sanctioned home (ci.yml microcopy job comment updated). The original L22 entry is annotated inline below.

**Closed by [edit] (Story 2.1):**

- **Story 2.1 binding deliverables (AC1–AC6)** — **Closed by [edit].** Landed: `packages/i18n/src/{locale,resolver,number,currency,classification,catalog,index}.ts` + `locales/{hi,en}/common.json` + `locales/classification.json` + `scripts/{lib,check-parity}.ts` + `README.md` + the 42-test vitest suite (locale precedence incl. hi fallback; resolver + interpolation + loud throws; numeral round-trips; currency lakh/crore + decimals + negative + hi-Devanagari + non-finite throw; classification parse/resolve/declare; parity pure-core hit/miss/member-vs-admin/empty-no-op). CI wiring: `turbo.json` `i18n:check-parity` task + root `i18n:check` passthrough + `i18n-parity` ci.yml job (between `tokens-theme-check` and `pii-scrape`) + `scripts/ci-local.sh` registration (static-job count corrected to 15 — the header had drifted to a stale "13" after cadence-check (AI-1) landed as the 14th without a header bump; i18n-parity is the 15th). Green-with-teeth: `common` defaults member-facing and is parity-enforced now; teeth proven end-to-end (en-only key → exit 1 naming it → removed → exit 0, tree clean). See ADR-0018.

**Resolved via explicit deferral (Story 2.1):**

- **Broader architecture §4328 i18n modules — `date.ts` / `relative-time.ts` / `pluralize.ts` / `actor-class-register.ts` / `per-pariwar/` string overlays** — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** §4328 sketches a fuller i18n module set; Story 2.1 deliberately lands only the AC-bound surface (resolver + locale + classification + parity) plus the three named numeral/currency functions (the L22 re-trigger). No surface consumes the remaining modules yet; building them now is premature plumbing (the "scaffolding with no consumer" trap). **Re-triggers (per module, at first consuming surface):** `date.ts` / `relative-time.ts` → the first surface rendering member-facing dates/relative times (Epic 3 member lifecycle / life-events panel); `pluralize.ts` → the first member-facing pluralized count string; `actor-class-register.ts` → the first per-actor-class copy variance (architecture §2867-2870, per-state copy + actor-class register); `per-pariwar/` overlays → second-Pariwar provisioning needing per-tenant string overrides.

At v1 there are NO consumers, so this is inert (`react` is an optional peer dep; the server-safe core — `t`, `getLocale`, numerals — is otherwise framework-agnostic). **Re-trigger:** the first non-React server consumer (e.g. `apps/api` for API copy/locale layering, architecture L1833-1834) imports the package — at that point split the React hooks into a `@twt/i18n/react` subpath export so the core loads without `react`.
- **Broad member-surface parity enforcement footprint** — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** `classification.json` `namespaces` is empty (no admin-only namespace yet) and the 1.17 microcopy `scope.copy_globs` is NOT widened at 2.1 (no member surface ships locale strings yet — the L24 broad-member-enforcement re-trigger is intentionally NOT fired here). Parity teeth currently bite the `common` member-facing namespace; they grow domain-by-domain as Epic 2+ member surfaces add `locales/**` strings, data-driven, no gate code change. **Re-trigger:** Epic 2+ member surfaces populate additional `locales/{hi,en}/{domain}.json` (and, for the microcopy register, member `copy_globs` per the existing L24 deferral).

## Story 1.17 — resolutions + new deferrals (design-system foundation author-commit, 2026-06-16)

Two epic-phrasing corrections are baked in: tokens home = `packages/tokens` (not `packages/ui`); "FM-1..FM-14 forensic-microcopy rules" = the seven UX-spec Failure-Mode commitments (the gate realizes the automatable subset; ADR-0016 records the mapping). Two decisions were LOCKED at create-story: (1) gate name `scripts/microcopy/`; (2) v1 footprint = forward-compat + a bounded allow-listed `apps/admin` scan (green-with-teeth).

**Closed by [edit] (Story 1.17):**

- **Story 1.17 binding deliverables** — **Closed by [edit].** Landed: (a) the `@twt/tokens` token registry (`src/tokens.ts` — semantic color aliases per §8 + general aliases + the firm admin status palette; the five UX-DR9 type-role faces + Latin pairings; the four named spacing tokens; the four border tokens, no shadow per FM-3; FM-14 #1 semantic-name invariant + #3 per-group comments; unit-tested in `tests/tokens.test.ts`); (e) the pure `renderThemeCss()` semantic CSS generator (`src/theme.ts`) → the committed `src/theme.css` + the FM-4 determinism gate (`scripts/check-theme-determinism.ts`, the `tokens:check-theme-determinism` turbo task, the `tokens-theme-check` ci.yml job); (b)+(c)+(d) the `scripts/microcopy/` gate (pure `lib.ts` + impure `check.ts` + 34 fixture tests + README + the root `microcopy.yaml` config + root `microcopy:check`/`microcopy:test` + the `microcopy` ci.yml job) enforcing FM-14 #2 magic-number colors + the vocabulary register + tone prohibitions + the amendment-A2 numeral discipline, every finding naming file+line+canonical replacement, green-with-teeth over the `apps/admin` slice (teeth proven end-to-end). - **`packages/ui` extraction (D4-1.11b second leg)** — **Resolved via explicit deferral.** `packages/ui` is reserved for TWT-data-shape **composed components** under the second-consumer promotion rule (architecture §Component-layer-boundary L4459-4463), which is unmet today — no composed component has a second consumer.
