## Deferred from: code review of 1-3-packages-events-event-log-primitive (2026-06-09)

- **W1: `replayState` unbounded stream load** [`packages/events/src/events-log.ts`] — `replayState` calls `loadEvents` with no upper-bound; large or long-lived streams (member lifecycle after years, Pool Engine streams) will materialize all rows into application memory causing OOM or multi-second latency. Design-level concern requiring API signature change (pagination / streaming / `maxEvents` cap). Deferred as beyond Story 1.3 scope; revisit at Story 3.1+ or Story 7.x when real stream sizes are known.

- **W2: `appendEvent` prototype-polluting payload keys** [`packages/events/src/events-log.ts`] — No key sanitization before INSERT. Downstream reducers that spread event payloads into state objects could be vulnerable to `__proto__` / `constructor` pollution from historical events. Optional `payloadSchema` is defense-in-depth; Story 1.4 Zod contracts and per-domain schemas (3.1+/6.x/7.x) are the right enforcement layer.

- **W3: Migration 0001 missing `SET LOCAL search_path` qualifier** [`packages/domain/migrations/0001_events-log.sql`] — Hand-supplemented trigger DDL relies on the session's default `search_path` (`public`). Consistent with migration 0000 pattern; low risk on standard Cloud SQL config. Revisit if multi-schema deployment is introduced.

- **W4: Concurrent-conflict test not truly parallel** [`packages/events/tests/append-event.test.ts`] — The `ConcurrencyError` test uses a single connection with SAVEPOINT to simulate a unique-violation rather than two concurrent connections. The spec says "two parallel `appendEvent` calls." The unique-constraint behavior is proven; true two-connection concurrency testing deferred to Story 1.6 when the Postgres CI service-container substrate lands. **RESOLVED 2026-06-11 per Story 1.6 closure** (artifact produced): the SAVEPOINT test was REPLACED with a true two-connection test — two pooled clients each run a full BEGIN→setPariwarScope→appendEvent→COMMIT unit in parallel via `Promise.allSettled`; exactly one wins (version 1), the other rejects `ConcurrencyError`. (Also fixed a latent pre-existing assertion in the same file — `rejects negative expectedVersion` matched a stale message string that the new live-DB CI surfaced.)

- **W5: `loadEvents` no `pariwarId` scoping** — already tracked at D9-1.3 (Story 1.6 RLS `pgPolicy`). This entry is a cross-reference note: `loadEvents` filters by `streamId` only; Story 1.6 adds the DB-level policy; application-layer guard deferred to that Story.

- **W6: `canonical-json` non-ASCII / Unicode key ordering tests absent** [`packages/events/tests/canonical-json.test.ts`] — All 10 test cases use ASCII keys. RFC 8785 key ordering for non-ASCII characters (Devanagari, extended Latin) is a future concern when real non-ASCII payload shapes are introduced. ADR-0004 forward-path tracks the subset boundary. Revisit at first non-ASCII payload Story (likely Story 2.x i18n or Story 3.x member data).

- **W7 (a.k.a. W7-1.3): UUID string validation in `loadEvents` / `appendEvent`** [`packages/events/src/events-log.ts`] — `streamId`, `pariwarId`, `actorId` are typed `string`; invalid UUIDs produce opaque Postgres errors (`invalid input syntax for type uuid`). Story 1.7 branded types (`packages/domain/src/ids/`) are the intended fix; at Story 1.3, callers are trusted to pass valid UUIDs. **UNBLOCKED but NOT addressed at Story 1.7 (per [[feedback_closure_language_precision]] — distinct from "resolved"):** the branded-ID substrate + smart constructors the fix depends on now exist (D12-1.4 closed), but Story 1.7's scope is `@twt/domain` / `@twt/contracts`; retrofitting `@twt/events` `loadEvents`/`appendEvent` signatures to brand `streamId`/`pariwarId`/`actorId` (`EventId`/stream brands too) is `@twt/events` territory and was explicitly left out (Story 1.7 Task 1.3 "skip the broad retroactive substitution"). Re-trigger: an `@twt/events`-touching story adopts the brands.

- **W8: `StateMachine` `transitions` table not validated against `reduce` function** [`packages/events/src/state-machine.ts`] — The `transitions` array is documentation-only; a `StateMachine` constructed with a `transitions` table that contradicts `reduce` produces no runtime error or warning. Low risk at Story 1.3 scope (no concrete state machine yet); revisit at Story 3.1+ when concrete member-state machine is authored.

## Deferred from: code review of 1-4-packages-contracts-zod-openapi-contract-scaffolding (2026-06-10)

- **F07: `check-openapi-determinism.ts` relative path robustness** [`packages/contracts/scripts/check-openapi-determinism.ts:22`] — Uses `'scripts/emit-openapi.ts'` as a string literal (relative to `cwd: packages/contracts`) rather than an absolute path via `path.resolve(here, 'emit-openapi.ts')`. Independent re-review of Groups B–E confirmed the prior `7823fe4` triage as thorough. These are residual items beyond that triage (the substantive miss, RD1/crypto:check compile assertion, is a decision-needed item tracked in the story file, not here).

- **RW1: KEK `rotation_period` set without `next_rotation_time`** [`infra/gcp/cloud-kms-dev.tf:37`] — The GCP provider may require `next_rotation_time` whenever `rotation_period` is set (registry docs inconclusive). Not exercised today — live `terraform apply` is deferred (D1-1.5). Verify against a real `terraform plan` at Story 1.15; if required, set a fixed RFC3339 `next_rotation_time` or use a `time_rotating` resource to compute it.

- **RW2: Negative-path test gaps beyond the TB-series** [`packages/domain/tests/encryption/envelope.test.ts`] — No test for envelope empty/oversized plaintext through `encryptTier1`, no bit-flipped-`ciphertext`-with-correct-AAD `decryptTier1` failure (distinct from the AAD-mismatch path TB9 covers), and `parseEnvelope` validates `iv`/`authTag` byte-lengths (TB4/TB5) but not `encryptedDek` length. Additive hardening; existing coverage is strong. Land when convenient or alongside the first per-row binding consumer (Story 3.1+).

- **RW3: `destroy_scheduled_duration` "30-day Cloud KMS platform max" claim unverified** [`infra/gcp/variables.tf`] — Documented as a hard cap in the variable, README, runbook §2.1, and ADR-0006; the actual Cloud KMS maximum is commonly cited as higher (up to ~120 days). 30 days is a valid conservative value (functionally fine), but the "platform max" framing is unverified and over-tightens the band. Verify the true maximum and correct the comment/validation if wrong.

- **RW4: `KMS_TEST_MODE=fake` CI env is inert** [`.github/workflows/ci.yml` crypto-check job] — No provider-selection code reads `KMS_TEST_MODE`; the tests construct `createFakeKmsProvider` directly, so the env var gates nothing today. Wire it to real provider selection at Story 1.15 (live toggle per TBW6), or drop it to avoid implying live-vs-fake coverage that does not exist.

## Story 1.6 deferred (substrate author-commit, 2026-06-11 per Decision 2026-06-11-042)

- **D1-1.6: Story 1.16a CI import-rule lint forbidding `pg.Pool.connect()` direct construction outside `packages/domain/src/cross-tenant/`** — architecture §1.2 line 739-740 + line 768-769 commits the rule; Story 1.6 commits the helper + module structural posture (single named call-site); Story 1.16a substantively wires the ESLint rule. Trigger: Story 1.16a dev-story start. **DISPOSITION (Story 1.16a, 2026-06-16): Closed by [edit]** — landed `@typescript-eslint/no-restricted-imports` on `pg` (allowTypeImports) in `eslint-config-twt` + carve-out + tests. **Scope deviation:** carve-out is role-based (`**/db.ts` + `**/test-utils/**` + tests; cwd-relative because the shared config runs per-package), not `cross-tenant/`-only (which constructs no pool — it receives them by param; `db.ts` is the real construction site). The narrower service-role-specific rule re-triggers with D9-1.6. See "Story 1.16a deferred + discharged" above + ADR-0012.

- **D2-1.6: Sentinel UUIDs in `runAsCrossTenant` audit event** (`00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` for both the audit-stream + the cross-tenant `pariwar_id` marker) — Story 1.10 substantively re-keys to a dedicated audit-stream UUID once `audit_log_entries` lands. Trigger: Story 1.10 dev-story start.

- **D3-1.6: Pariwar-Passport carve-out RLS policies** (cross-Pariwar-readable per architecture §1.2 line 726-729) — Story 1.7 substantively authors `pariwar-passport-rls.ts` with cross-readable policies, reviewed together with the scoped policies. Trigger: Story 1.7 dev-story start. **RESOLVED 2026-06-11 per Story 1.7 closure (Closed by [edit])**: `packages/domain/src/policies/pariwar-passport-rls.ts` declares `pariwar_passport_cross_readable_select` (`USING (true)`) + `pariwar_passport_tenant_isolation_write` (`FOR ALL`, Story 1.6 closed-failure construct); migration `0003` ENABLE+FORCE RLS + GRANT SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE (no DELETE); the adversarial leak suite asserts the Passport as the expected cross-readable exception while every scoped table still returns 0 rows; reviewed together with the scoped policies in `policies/`. ADR-0007 records the read-cross/write-isolated asymmetry. Decision 2026-06-11-043.

- **D4-1.6: Apps/api scope-resolution middleware** (architecture §2.5 line 1449-1461; consumes `setPariwarScope` at handler entry inside a request-scoped transaction) — Story 1.9 substantively authors `apps/api/src/middleware/scope-resolution/`. Trigger: Story 1.9 dev-story start.

- **D5-1.6: Substantive `audit_log_entries` integration for `runAsCrossTenant` cross-tenant audit emission** — Story 1.6 emits an `audit.cross_tenant_access` event into `events_log` via a direct drizzle INSERT (NOT `@twt/events.appendEvent`, which would invert the package layering / create a turbo cycle); Story 1.10 wires the substantive `audit_log_entries` row with hash-chain + 6h off-site mirror. Trigger: Story 1.10 dev-story start.

- **D6-1.6: Per-tenant TanStack Query key isolation** (architecture §4.2 line 2483-2491; query keys carry `pariwar_id` to prevent cross-tenant cache pollution) — apps/admin + apps/mobile Story territory (1.9+ for admin; 3.x for mobile). Trigger: per-app Story dev-story start.

- **D7-1.6: `setPariwarScope` SQL-injection-surface hardening** — the implementation uses single-quote interpolation guarded by `UUID_REGEX` upstream-validation (`SET LOCAL` does not reliably accept bind parameters across pg versions); a future hardening could use parameter binding once verified. Captured for defense-in-depth even though the current implementation is safe.

- **D8-1.6: Postgres service-container CI job per-step caching** — the `integration-tests` job boots a fresh Postgres + re-applies migrations every run (no data-volume / schema cache). Story 1.16a friction-budget territory if CI duration becomes a long-pole.

- **D9-1.6: `runAsCrossTenant` service-pool credential separation for production** — `SET LOCAL row_security = off` requires superuser or `BYPASSRLS`. In local Docker + CI `twt_dev_app = POSTGRES_USER = implicit superuser`, so it works (and the RLS-enforcement tests `SET ROLE twt_app` to shed that superuser). Against Cloud SQL production, `twt_dev_app` is a standard non-superuser login role and `SET LOCAL row_security = off` raises `permission denied`; a separate service-role pool (a `twt_service`-login role carrying `BYPASSRLS`, distinct from the application pool) is required. The `runAsCrossTenant` signature gains a `servicePool` parameter at that point. The migration's dev/CI `GRANT twt_service TO twt_dev_app` is dev/CI-only — production wires a distinct login role. Trigger: Story 1.10 + Story 1.16a, when `runAsCrossTenant` is first exercised against a real Cloud SQL instance.

- **D10-1.6: Migration-time BYPASSRLS self-test only fires at fresh apply** — drizzle skips already-applied migrations (consulting `__drizzle_migrations`), so re-running `pnpm db:migrate` does NOT re-execute migration 0002's self-test. The self-test therefore guards a *fresh-environment apply* (a new deploy where `twt_app` somehow pre-exists with BYPASSRLS), not a continuous drift on an established DB. A continuous BYPASSRLS-posture monitor (e.g., a periodic `pg_roles` audit job) is downstream-ops territory. Captured for accuracy; the self-test as written is the cheapest schema-layer detector and fires correctly at apply.

- **D11-1.6: Terraform `lifecycle.precondition` block on `google_sql_user.app` skipped** [`infra/gcp/cloud-sql-dev.tf`] — AC-8 prescribes a `lifecycle { precondition { ... } }` block asserting the Cloud SQL login role carries `NOBYPASSRLS` (Terraform declarative enforcement); the implementation skips it because the precondition requires reading Terraform provider state that is only available after the Cloud SQL instance is substantively provisioned. Add the precondition at Story 1.15 live provisioning when the `google_sql_database_instance` resource has real outputs to assert against. Trigger: Story 1.15 dev-story.

## Deferred from: code review of 1-6-pariwar-id-first-class-rls-adversarial-test (2026-06-11)

- **W1-CR1.6: `fn()` throws after `SET LOCAL row_security = off` — no audit trail for partial cross-tenant reads** [`packages/domain/src/cross-tenant/run-as-cross-tenant.ts:82-110`] — If `fn()` reads cross-tenant data and then throws before the audit INSERT executes, ROLLBACK removes all traces of the access. The audit-trail guarantee requires Story 1.10 hardened semantics (e.g., pre-commit audit hook or separate audit connection). Trigger: Story 1.10 dev-story.

- **W2-CR1.6: `twt_service` has INSERT grant but no matching permissive RLS policy (deny-all in practice)** [`packages/domain/migrations/0002_events-log-rls.sql`] — `GRANT SELECT, INSERT ON events_log TO twt_service` is vestigial: `ENABLE + FORCE RLS` with no permissive policy bound to `twt_service` = deny-all. The intended production path is `SET LOCAL row_security = off` via a BYPASSRLS service pool (D9-1.6). Consider adding an explanatory comment to the migration so the intent is clear. Trigger: Story 1.10 dev-story (substantive twt_service wiring).

- **W3-CR1.6: ENABLE RLS could commit without FORCE if drizzle-kit processes breakpoints in autocommit** [`packages/domain/migrations/0002_events-log-rls.sql`] — If drizzle-kit executes each `-->statement-breakpoint`-separated statement as a separate auto-committed batch (rather than inside one outer transaction), `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` could commit while a subsequent `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` fails, leaving the table owner able to bypass RLS. Follows the Story 1.3 precedent. Verify drizzle-kit's transaction wrapping behavior for DDL at Story 1.15 live provisioning.

- **W4-CR1.6: `withPariwarScope` tested in RLS-bypass mode — CI superuser bypasses FORCE RLS** [`packages/domain/src/db.ts`] — The production `withPariwarScope` API is never tested with RLS actually active in this diff; `twt_dev_app` is a Docker/CI superuser and bypasses RLS regardless of `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`. The policy-regression and adversarial tests explicitly call `SET LOCAL ROLE twt_app` to shed superuser; `withPariwarScope` itself does not. Related to D3-1.6 (FORCE RLS behavioral test impossibility with superuser CI).

- **W5-CR1.6: turbo cache — integration test results could be served stale on CI re-runs** [`turbo.json`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`] — `DATABASE_URL` is now part of the turbo cache key, but DB schema state (e.g., whether migrations have run) is not. A zero-output cached result (all tests skipped) could be replayed on a subsequent run with the same connection string. Low risk with ephemeral service containers where each run gets a fresh DB. Revisit if Vercel Remote Cache or a self-hosted turbo cache is configured.

- **W6-CR1.6: Audit payload caller-controlled — `reason`/`pariwarIds`/`actorId` unconstrained** [`packages/domain/src/cross-tenant/run-as-cross-tenant.ts`] — No schema validation or allowlist on the audit row's `reason`, `pariwarIds`, or `actorId` fields. For a "future auditors filter by reason" audit trail, unconstrained free text is an audit-log poisoning surface. `runAsCrossTenant` is currently an internal API called by trusted code only; validation belongs at Story 1.10 audit hardening.

- **W7-CR1.6: `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public` missing for `twt_app` role** [`packages/domain/migrations/0002_events-log-rls.sql`] — In hardened production environments where `PUBLIC` USAGE on the `public` schema is revoked, `SET LOCAL ROLE twt_app` sessions will fail with "permission denied for schema public" before any RLS policy even runs. Passes on Docker/CI defaults. Add `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO twt_app, twt_service` in the migration or address at Story 1.15 production hardening.

- **W8-CR1.6: `CROSS_TENANT_SENTINEL_UUID` hot-row contention** [`packages/domain/src/cross-tenant/run-as-cross-tenant.ts`] — Every `runAsCrossTenant` call reads and writes the same `stream_id = CROSS_TENANT_SENTINEL_UUID` row for `MAX(event_version)`, creating a hot-row lock contention point under concurrent load. Also creates a monotonically growing event chain with no rotation. This concern is resolved as a side effect of the P1 race-condition fix (if the fix adopts `gen_random_uuid()` per call, each audit event gets its own stream at version 1). If P1 instead uses `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, this becomes a production scale concern at Story 1.10+.

- **W9-CR1.6: `setPariwarScope` lacks runtime transaction-active guard** [`packages/domain/src/db.ts`] — `SET LOCAL` silently degrades to connection-scoped `SET` when called outside a transaction, leaking `pariwarId` to the next pooled request. A `pg_current_xact_id_if_assigned() IS NOT NULL` guard would surface the misuse at the cost of a round-trip per `setPariwarScope` call. Story 1.9 (scope-resolution middleware wiring) is the natural gate for validating this invariant in production; deferred to avoid the round-trip overhead until the call site is established.

- **W10-CR1.6: `@twt/domain/policies` subpath import not in exports map** [`packages/domain/package.json`] — AC-1 specifies `import { … } from '@twt/domain/policies'` as the canonical import path; no `exports` map entry registers this subpath. Deep imports (`@twt/domain/src/policies/index.js`) resolve currently under the project's TypeScript `moduleResolution` settings but will break with `ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED` if a strict exports map is added. Register `"./policies": "./src/policies/index.js"` in `package.json` `exports` at Story 1.16c. **UPDATE (Story 1.16c, 2026-06-16): Resolved via explicit deferral** (BigDev re-defer decision, Dev Notes → Decision 2) — coupled with W12-CR1.6; a *partial* exports map (just `"./policies"`) without a `"./src/test-utils/*"` wildcard would itself break W12, so both land together in a focused `@twt/domain` exports-map hardening pass. Orthogonal to the FR-100 gate. See the 1.16c section above.

- **W11-CR1.6: `GRANT twt_service TO twt_dev_app` requires ADMIN OPTION in non-superuser production** [`packages/domain/migrations/0002_events-log-rls.sql`] — In production Cloud SQL, the migration executor may not have ADMIN OPTION on the `twt_service` role, causing the `GRANT` statement to fail mid-migration with "must be superuser or have ADMIN OPTION". In local Docker + CI, `twt_dev_app` is a superuser and the grant succeeds. Ensure the production migration executor has sufficient privilege, or restructure the grant to use a superuser-owned role at Story 1.15 live provisioning.

- **W12-CR1.6: Deep internal path import from `@twt/events` into `@twt/domain` test-utils bypasses future exports map** [`packages/events/tests/integration-setup.ts`] — The shim imports `../../packages/domain/src/test-utils/integration-setup.js` directly via a relative path that reaches inside the package. If `@twt/domain` ever adds an `exports` map (without a `"./src/test-utils/*"` wildcard), this import will break with `ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED`. Update to a registered subpath or top-level re-export when the exports map is added at Story 1.16c. **UPDATE (Story 1.16c, 2026-06-16): Resolved via explicit deferral** (BigDev re-defer decision, Dev Notes → Decision 2) — coupled with W10-CR1.6 (both must land together: a partial exports map without a `"./src/test-utils/*"` wildcard would cause this very break). Orthogonal to the FR-100 gate. **Re-trigger:** a focused `@twt/domain` exports-map hardening pass. See the 1.16c section above.

Design choice for current scope. **Trigger: any new second-factor method added to the admin auth flow.**
- **CR-A-4: `recovery_codes.codeHash` no per-user uniqueness constraint** — collision probability is negligible with proper CSPRNG code generation; the `consumed_at` burn-on-use path handles the consumed row correctly. **Trigger: if code generation ever diverges from CSPRNG (test code using sequential values, etc.).**
- **CR-A-5: `seedUser` test helper passes non-UUID `id` without early validation** — would throw `InvalidBrandedIdError` inside `toUserId()` with a potentially confusing error message when FK failures are actually the root cause. Test infra only, no prod risk. **Trigger: a debugging session confused by the error message.**

- **D3-1.7: Distributed branding cache (Redis) replacing the in-process `Map`** [`packages/domain/src/pariwar-passport/read.ts`] — Story 1.7 ships an in-process cache-aside honouring `BRANDING_BUNDLE_MAX_STALENESS_MS = 60_000` (architecture §1.10 line 1047-1048). Redis is explicitly a future trigger (§1.10 line 1077). **Trigger: the §1.10 distributed-cache trigger fires** (multi-instance API + cache-coherence pressure). - **D4-1.7: FK on `pariwar_passport.created_by`** [`packages/domain/src/schema/pariwar_passport.ts`] — `created_by` is an unconstrained nullable `uuid` (no FK) because the admin-users table does not exist yet. **Trigger: Story 1.9+** (admin users table lands); add the FK retroactively then.

## Deferred from: code review of 1-9-admin-authentication (Group B — API Framework, 2026-06-12)

- **CR-B-1: `scope.change` audit flood** — `scopeResolutionHook` emits `scope.change` on every scoped request, not only on scope transitions; AC-9 §2.5 intent is transition events. Producing one audit line per request makes the audit trail unqueryable for anomaly detection. Fix requires explicit scope-change detection (compare actorId + pariwarId against the previous scope in session). Deferred: architectural, needs spec clarification on rate-limiting or sampling semantics.
- **CR-B-2: `SESSION_SECRET` entropy not code-enforceable** — 32-char length check passes for all-spaces or known public example values. True entropy validation (Shannon, charset checks) adds complexity without guarantees. Resolved via explicit deferral: operational concern; remediated at the deployment level (Secret Manager + rotation policy). Re-trigger: deployment hardening / secret-rotation policy.
- **CR-B-3: `AuthorizationDeniedError`/`ApiError` class hierarchy in `errorMappingHandler`** — if `AuthorizationDeniedError` extends `ApiError` the `ApiError` branch fires first, bypassing the dedicated 403 handler. Depends on the `@twt/domain` class hierarchy (unverified in this review). Resolved via explicit deferral: low risk if `AuthorizationDeniedError` does NOT extend `ApiError`; verify at Group C review time.

## Story 1.10 — resolutions + new deferrals (substrate author-commit, 2026-06-12 per Decision 2026-06-12-046)

Per [[feedback_closure_language_precision]]: "**Closed by [edit]**" = the artifact the deferral predicted was produced; "**Resolved via explicit deferral**" = the gap remains intentional with a recorded trigger.

### Closed by [edit] at Story 1.10

- **D2-1.9 (AuthAuditSink → real sink)** — Closed: `apps/api/src/audit/audit-log-sink.ts` `createAuditLogSink` maps `AuthAuditEvent` → `writeAuditEntry`, swapped into `createDeps`.
- **D2-1.8 (rbac `onAuthorizationDenied` sink)** — Closed: routes through the same `deps.auditSink.emit`; lit up by the D2-1.9 swap (no rbac code touched). `scope.change` emission likewise.
- **D10-1.5 (KmsProvider.auditHook)** — Closed: `createKmsAuditHook` populated + wired in `createDeps`; KEK wrap/unwrap + blind-index HMAC emit audit lines.
- **D5-1.6 / D2-1.6 (cross-tenant audit re-key)** — Closed: `runAsCrossTenant` emits into `audit_log_entries` via `writeAuditEntry` (events_log placeholder removed; sentinel pariwar_id retained).
- **D9-1.6 (service-pool credential separation)** — Closed (shape): `runAsCrossTenant` gains the `servicePool` param; `AppDeps.servicePool` exposed; migration 0007 documents the prod `twt_service`-login BYPASSRLS posture. (Live prod credential = Resolved via explicit deferral, below.)
- **D7-1.3 (audit-log hash-chain consumer wiring)** — Closed: `audit_log_entries` table + `writeAuditEntry` producer + `verifyChainSegment` (the chain Story 1.11a verifies).
- **D13-1.2 (audit-log/integrity-check test slot)** — Closed: `tests/integration/audit-log/integrity-check.spec.ts` (8 live-DB tests incl. synthetic tamper).
- **D13-1.4 (audit transport contract — audit leg)** — Closed: `AuditLogEntryContract` (`.strict()`, standalone, OpenAPI `AuditLogEntry` component).
- **D13-1.5 (canonical-JSON consolidation)** — Closed via path (a): `canonical-json.ts` moved to `@twt/domain`, re-exported by `@twt/events`. (The optional `encryption/canonical-context.ts` delegation sub-leg = Resolved via explicit deferral, below.)
- **D3-1.3 (`audit.*` event-type registry leg)** — Closed by decision: audit lines are NOT double-written as `events_log` rows (Task 6.3); `audit_log_entries` is the canonical separately-retained store, so NO `audit.*` registry entries are added. The other registry legs (`member.*`/`claim.*`/…) remain per their own stories.
- **W16 (pg_partman on Cloud SQL PG16)** — Closed (verified + recorded): `pg_partman` is on the Cloud SQL for PostgreSQL supported-extensions list (PG 12-16); absent from the local `postgres:16-alpine` image (irrelevant — partitioning deferred). Native declarative partitioning needs no pg_partman anyway. Shipping non-partitioned (DD-4).
- **W2-CR1.6 (twt_service grant vs no policy)** — Closed: migration 0007 carries the explanatory comment — `twt_service` writes via the BYPASSRLS prod login, hence grants but no permissive write policy.
- **W8-CR1.6 (sentinel hot-row contention)** — Closed: the writer serializes on `pg_advisory_xact_lock(AUDIT_CHAIN_LOCK_KEY)`, not a shared sentinel row.
- **W6-CR1.6 (audit payload unconstrained / poisoning)** — Closed: `writeAuditEntry` validates input with a `.strict()` Zod schema (bounded lengths, dotted action, SHA-256-hex requestPayloadHash) at the boundary.
- **W1-CR1.6 (cross-tenant audit durability)** — Closed: `runAsCrossTenant` two-transaction split — the audit line commits FIRST (own advisory-lock tx), so a thrown `fn()` cannot erase it; fails closed if the audit write fails.

### Resolved via explicit deferral (gap intentional; trigger recorded)

- **D1-1.10: Live `terraform apply` of `infra/gcp/audit-mirror.tf` + the irreversible retention LOCK** — committed the IaC shape only (Story 1.5 D1-1.5 precedent). `var.enable_retention_lock` defaults false. **Trigger: production audit-mirror provisioning** (mirror project created + billing-linked); set `enable_retention_lock = true` after verifying the 7-year period.
- **D2-1.10: 6-hourly cron + durable watermark store** — the mirror push fn + CLI ship now; the 6h trigger + a durable `audit_mirror_state` watermark are wired with **pg-boss (Story 1.12)** (~~pg-boss not installed yet~~). v1 seeds the watermark from `AUDIT_MIRROR_SINCE_SEQ`. **Re-affirmed deferred (Story 1.12, DD-4):** the **pg-boss substrate now EXISTS (Story 1.12)** — but the 6h mirror cron was **not graduated** here because it still needs a durable `audit_mirror_state` watermark table + prod creds (graduating it would smuggle that scope in the back door). **Re-trigger (updated):** durable watermark table + prod mirror creds; then wire as a `boss.schedule(...)` cron in `apps/jobs/src/boot.ts` following the idempotency-vacuum precedent.
- **D3-1.10: Production `SERVICE_DATABASE_URL` (twt_service-login BYPASSRLS) credential** — the service pool reuses the app pool in dev/CI; the distinct prod login credential is Terraform/Secret-Manager. **Trigger: Cloud SQL deploy (Story 1.15) / first prod audit write.**
- **D4-1.10: `audit_log_entries` table partitioning (DD-4)** — ships non-partitioned (events_log precedent); the chain is over `seq` (partition-agnostic). **Trigger: a scale story (170M-row sizing, §1.5 P2).**
- **D5-1.10: `encryption/canonical-context.ts` → shared canonicalizer (D13-1.5 cleanup sub-leg)** — left untouched: it defines `encryptionContextAad` (a scoped AAD helper), not a second `canonicalJsonStringify`, so the "one canonicalizer" invariant already holds; re-deriving AES-GCM AAD risks making stored PII undecryptable. **Trigger: a dedicated encryption refactor with a ciphertext-roundtrip test.**
- **W-USAGE-1.10 (local test-DB schema USAGE):** the Story-1.6 deferred "USAGE ON SCHEMA missing" surfaced when a manual `DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE` local reset stripped twt_app's default `PUBLIC` USAGE (CI's fresh container is unaffected). **Trigger: if a migration ever recreates the public schema, add an explicit `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public`.**

## Deferred from: code review of 1-10-tamper-evident-audit-log-hash-chain-6h-off-site-mirror (2026-06-14)

Domain layer chunk reviewed (Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor). 4 patch + 1 decision-needed items are action items in the story file. The following were deferred as pre-existing:

- **CR-D1-1.10: `canonical-context.ts` second RFC 8785 implementation inside `@twt/domain`** — `packages/domain/src/encryption/canonical-context.ts` has a second hand-rolled canonical-JSON serializer (scoped to AES-GCM AAD derivation). It predates DD-1 and was explicitly deferred in Task 1.4: re-deriving the AAD bytes risks making stored Tier-1/Tier-2 PII undecryptable. **Trigger: a dedicated encryption refactor with full ciphertext-roundtrip testing.** (Same as D5-1.10 above.)

- **CR-D2-1.10: `verifyChainSegment` no programmatic predecessor-linkage signal** — for a single-row segment or any segment whose first row is not genesis, the function cannot verify that `row[0].prevAuditHash` matches its true predecessor. Returns `chainValid: true` with no flag. A `predecessorLinkageVerified` field on `ChainVerificationResult` would expose this to 1.11a callers. **Trigger: Story 1.11a integrity-check job implementation.** (`packages/domain/src/audit/hash-chain.ts`)

- **CR-D3-1.10: `timestamptz` ISO format contract not pinned for cross-language verifiers** — the hash over `recordedAt.toISOString()` is byte-stable within JS/node-postgres (millisecond precision, no microseconds). A future Go/Python verifier formatting `recorded_at` with microseconds would fail every hash check. Millisecond-precision contract should be documented in ADR-0004. **Trigger: any non-JS implementation of the integrity-check verifier.** (`packages/domain/src/audit/hash-chain.ts:auditRowDigestInput`)

- **CR-D4-1.10: Nil UUID not blocked in `auditEntryInputSchema`** — `z.string().uuid()` accepts `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000`. A real pariwar provisioned with the nil UUID would see all cross-tenant sentinel audit rows via the RLS policy. Fix belongs at pariwar provisioning (prevent assigning the nil UUID as a pariwar_id), not at the audit write boundary. **Trigger: pariwar provisioning story.** (`packages/domain/src/audit/write.ts:auditEntryInputSchema`)

- **CR-D5-1.10: `Function` values in object properties serialize as `'{}'` in `canonicalJsonStringify`** — diverges silently from `JSON.stringify` (which omits function-valued keys). No current caller exercises this path; all audit/event payloads are plain data. **Trigger: if any caller passes objects with function properties.** (`packages/domain/src/canonical-json.ts:canonicalize()`)

- **CR-D6-1.10: Hash field format not constrained in `AuditLogEntryContract`** — `requestPayloadHash`, `auditHash`, and `prevAuditHash` use `z.string().min(1)` in the wire contract; the domain writer enforces `/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/i`. Read contract is intentionally permissive (DB data is always valid), but cross-language chain verifiers (Go, Python) need the exact 64-char lowercase hex format pinned to avoid silently accepting malformed hashes. **Trigger: Story 1.11a cross-language verifier implementation or ADR-0004 amendment.** (`packages/contracts/src/audit/audit-log-entry.ts`)

- **CR-D7-1.10: `seq` missing `format: int64` in OpenAPI component** — the emitter produces `type: integer` with no format annotation; OpenAPI code generators default to `int32` (max 2,147,483,647 ≈ 2.1×10⁹). The actual write ceiling is `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` (~9×10¹⁵). Generated SDK clients in int32-defaulting generators will silently cap `seq` values above ~2.1B, producing wrong sort order in chain verification. **Trigger: Story 1.11b client SDK generation or any external SDK consumer.** (`openapi/v1.yaml:AuditLogEntry.seq`, `packages/contracts/src/audit/audit-log-entry.ts`)

- **CR-D8-1.10: Non-UUID `actorId` string causes silent audit write failure** — `AuthAuditEvent.actorId` is typed `string | null` with no UUID format constraint. If a future caller passes a non-UUID string (email, service name, enrollment token subject), `writeAuditEntry`'s `z.string().uuid()` rejects it at the Zod boundary; the fire-and-forget `.catch` absorbs it silently. Current callers all pass UUIDs. Fix: add a UUID format check in `authEventToAuditInput` so invalid actorId values are detected at the mapping layer with a diagnostic log, rather than being opaquely dropped at the Zod layer. **Trigger: any new non-UUID actor introduction.** (`apps/api/src/audit/audit-log-sink.ts:authEventToAuditInput`)

- **CR-D10-1.10: `recordedAt` → ISO string in mirror JSONL; Story 1.11a verifier must reconstitute a `Date`** — `serializeRow` writes `recordedAt: row.recordedAt.toISOString()` (a string). When Story 1.11a reads JSONL via `JSON.parse`, `recordedAt` is a `string`. But `verifyChainSegment` takes `AuditLogEntryRow[]` and internally calls `entry.recordedAt.toISOString()`. At runtime this throws `TypeError: toISOString is not a function`. The 1.11a verifier must reconstitute `new Date(parsed.recordedAt)` before calling `verifyChainSegment`. This producer-consumer contract is currently undocumented. **Trigger: Story 1.11a integrity-check job implementation.** (`apps/jobs/src/audit/mirror.ts:serializeRow`, `packages/domain/src/audit/hash-chain.ts`)

- **CR-D11-1.10: Seq gaps in mirror segment files — Story 1.11a must not assume seq contiguity** — PostgreSQL `GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY` sequences are not gap-free (rolled-back transactions burn seqs without inserting rows). Segment files may span non-contiguous seqs (e.g., `segment-1-5000.jsonl` followed by `segment-5007-10000.jsonl` if seqs 5001–5006 were burned). The hash chain remains valid (prevAuditHash links seq 5007 → seq 5000). Story 1.11a verifier must not infer "missing rows" from the gap; missing seqs are expected and benign. **Trigger: Story 1.11a spec and verifier implementation.** (`apps/jobs/src/audit/mirror.ts:pushNewAuditLinesToMirror`)

- **CR-D12-1.10: `prevent_destroy = true` unconditional — no Terraform escape-hatch for dev/staging teardown** — Terraform `lifecycle { prevent_destroy = true }` cannot be conditioned on input variables (HCL limitation); the block is hardcoded on `google_storage_bucket.audit_mirror`. A `terraform destroy` on dev or staging requires removing the block from source before running. This is the same pattern used for KMS resources in this repo (`cloud-kms-dev.tf`). Operator runbook must document the manual removal step for ephemeral environment teardown. **No code change available at the Terraform layer.** **Trigger: runbook for ephemeral environment teardown; Story 1.15 staging/prod rollout.** (`infra/gcp/audit-mirror.tf`)

- **CR-D13-1.10: Mirror bucket co-located in same region as primary — no independent `audit_mirror_region` variable** — The aliased `google.audit_mirror` provider and bucket `location` both use `var.region` (architecture-frozen `asia-south1`). A regional GCS outage would affect both the primary audit log and the off-site mirror simultaneously, reducing disaster-recovery value. Architecture §5.1 freezes `asia-south1` for v1; introducing a separate region variable is acceptable but out of scope until staging/prod expansion. **Trigger: Story 1.15 (staging + prod infrastructure expansion).** (`infra/gcp/audit-mirror.tf`, `infra/gcp/variables.tf`)

## Deferred from: code review of 1-14-rate-limiting-login-wall-forced-pagination-honeypot-noindex (2026-06-15)

- **CR-D1-1.14: Audit dedup key is actor-scoped, not route-scoped** — `makeRateLimitAuditEmitter` uses `bucketKey = ${key}:${windowBucket}` where `key` is the rate-limit key (actor/IP). If the same actor trips two different named rate-limit routes in the same 1-minute window, only the first trip's `rate_limit.exceeded` audit line is emitted; subsequent route trips are deduped. The `routeUrl` in the single line reflects the first trip only. By-spec ("one line per key per window") but loses per-route granularity. **Trigger: future story needing per-route audit visibility; fix = key on `${key}:${routeUrl}:${bucket}`.** (`apps/api/src/plugins/rate-limit/index.ts:makeRateLimitAuditEmitter`)

- **CR-D2-1.14: `emitted` dedup Map has no size cap** — The lazy-prune evicts entries from `> WINDOW_MS * 2` ago, but entries within the current and last window accumulate unboundedly. Under high-cardinality DDoS (thousands of distinct IPs each tripping once), the Map grows O(unique keys / 2-min window). The code comment claims "memory stays bounded" — that is incorrect for the current window. Acceptable at bootstrap (single-instance + Cloudflare Layer-1). **Trigger: Add-Redis trigger (§1.4 L806) / multi-instance deploy; add a `MAX_MAP_SIZE` guard or switch to a TTL-evicting store.** (`apps/api/src/plugins/rate-limit/index.ts:makeRateLimitAuditEmitter`)

- **CR-D3-1.14: `search` and `write` named thresholds defined but not applied to any route** — `namedRateLimits(deps)` exports `{ read, search, write }` but only `.read` is consumed (audit-list route). No Epic 1 search or write endpoints exist. Config values `SEARCH_RATE_MAX` / `WRITE_RATE_MAX` are dead at runtime. **Trigger: first search endpoint (Epic 3 member directory / Sahyog archive) and first write endpoint (Epic 3+ mutations).** (`apps/api/src/plugins/rate-limit/index.ts:namedRateLimits`)

- **CR-D4-1.14: `paginatedResponse` cursor-shape assertion absent from forced-pagination guard** — `forced-pagination.spec.ts` asserts bounded `limit` param on every collection GET but does NOT assert the 200 body matches the `paginatedResponse({ items[], cursor? })` shape (AC-3: "guard asserts … a paginatedResponse-shaped body"). The only Epic 1 list endpoint uses a bare `z.array()` response (not a `paginatedResponse()` wrapper), so the shape clause cannot be asserted yet. **Trigger: first `paginatedResponse()`-shaped public list endpoint (Epic 3+); add `isPaginatedResponseShape()` detector to the guard test.** (`apps/api/tests/integration/forced-pagination.spec.ts`)

- **CR-D5-1.14: Forced-pagination guard validates OpenAPI schema `maximum` declaration, not runtime enforcement** — `declaresBoundedLimit()` checks the OpenAPI output for a finite `maximum` field; it does not send `?limit=cap+1` to each collection endpoint. A future endpoint could declare `maximum: 200` in OpenAPI without adding Zod `.max(200)`, and the guard would pass while the server silently accepts any value. Current single endpoint (audit-list) has a behavioral test covering this case. **Trigger: each new list endpoint registered; mitigation = include a `?limit=cap+1 → 400` assertion in the behavioral test for that route.** (`apps/api/tests/integration/forced-pagination.spec.ts:declaresBoundedLimit`)

- **CR-D6-1.14: No test asserts `loginRateMax` / `stepUpRateMax` per-route limits still trip** — Both per-route limits are preserved in config and route registrations (`LOGIN_RATE`, `stepUpRate`), but the test suites set these to 100000 so they never fire in CI. The only behavioral coverage for per-route limit tripping is the `READ_RATE_MAX` test in `rate-limit.spec.ts`. Pre-existing gap. **Trigger: no specific trigger; consider adding a `LOGIN_RATE_MAX=2` low-ceiling hermetic test analogous to the `READ_RATE_MAX=3` case.** (`apps/api/tests/integration/rate-limit.spec.ts`)

- **CR-D7-1.14: Minor — honeypot audit context `path` field contains full URL (query string included); `limit=201` boundary not tested** — (a) `context.path: request.url` in `honeypotHandler` includes query strings; the field name implies a path-only value. Rename to `url` or document. (b) `forced-pagination.spec.ts` uses `limit=99999` for the over-cap test; `limit=201` (one over the admin-tier max of 200) is a tighter boundary assertion. Low priority. **Trigger: next audit-context schema normalization pass.** (`apps/api/src/plugins/security-headers/index.ts:honeypotHandler`, `apps/api/tests/integration/forced-pagination.spec.ts`)

## Deferred from: code review of 1-17-design-system-foundation-tokens-typography-vocabulary-numeral-hardening.md (Round 2, 2026-06-17)

Review layers: Blind Hunter ✓ · Edge Case Hunter ✓ · Acceptance Auditor ✓. 0 decision-needed · 6 patches applied (P1 isAllowed zero-width guard; P2 exit(1) for declared-but-empty code_globs; P3 tokens.test.ts hex regex {3,8}→proper-alternation; P4 FUNCTIONAL_COLOR extend lookbehind (?<![.\[]) to exclude Tailwind bg-[rgba(...)]; P5 resolveGlobs per-glob warning on nonexistent base dir; P6 report→Sahyog Vivran positive fixture test) · 0 dismissed; 4 deferred items below.

- **`INLINE_HINDI_FORMAT` misses `hi-u-nu-deva` and `numberingSystem: 'devanagari'` locale-extension forms** — the pattern only matches the BCP-47 base tag `hi` or the `Devanagari` script subtag; a caller using the multi-extension form `'hi-u-nu-deva'` or the `Intl.NumberFormat` option `{ numberingSystem: 'devanagari' }` escapes the check entirely. Forward-compat gap: no member surfaces exist at v1 (`copy_globs` is empty), so the check is a no-op in all live code paths today. Re-trigger: Epic 2+ member surfaces land and `copy_globs` is populated, OR any admin code uses an `Intl.NumberFormat` call with a Devanagari numbering-system option. [`scripts/microcopy/lib.ts INLINE_HINDI_FORMAT`]
- **Duplicate findings when a file matches both `code_globs` and `copy_globs`** — if a file path is returned by both `resolveGlobs(config.scope.codeGlobs)` and `resolveGlobs(config.scope.copyGlobs)`, the gate scans it twice (once as a code file, once as a copy file), producing duplicate findings for the same violation. Impossible at v1 (`copy_globs: []`); a latent correctness bug. Re-trigger: Epic 2+ populates `copy_globs` — add a set-difference dedup (`copyFiles = copyFiles.filter(f => !codeFileSet.has(f))`) before the scan loops. [`scripts/microcopy/check.ts`]
- **`checkNumerals` fires on Devanagari digits in source-code comments** — the numeral check is line-based regex; a developer comment like `// ४ results shown` in a `.tsx` file under `apps/admin` would be flagged as a violation even though it is not member-visible copy. Requires a parser-level (AST) fix to distinguish comment spans from JSX text / string literals. The allow-list in `microcopy.yaml` is the v1 escape hatch for any genuine false-positive. Re-trigger: if a false-positive is hit in admin source comments during development — either add an allow-list entry or invest in an AST-based numeral checker at that point. [`scripts/microcopy/lib.ts checkNumerals`]
- **`check-theme-determinism.ts` byte-compare breaks on UTF-8 BOM** — `Buffer.compare` is byte-exact; if the committed `theme.css` or the regenerated output ever gains a BOM (three-byte sequence `EF BB BF`), the comparison fails with a false-negative even when the CSS content is identical. Extremely unlikely in this macOS/Linux-native monorepo (git normalizes line endings; no Windows-authored files exist yet). Re-trigger: first Windows contributor pushes a file via an editor that inserts a BOM (VS Code on Windows with `"files.encoding": "utf8bom"`). Fix: strip the BOM from both buffers before comparing, or add a `.editorconfig` `charset = utf-8` (no BOM) rule. [`packages/tokens/scripts/check-theme-determinism.ts`]

Spec-consistent behaviour (AC-2 explicitly limits PII detection to public renders); risk materialises at Story 2.5/11a.2 when the live-render integration spec (`scrape-test.spec.ts`) constructs `RenderSnapshot` objects. **Trigger: Story 2.5 / Epic 11a.2 live-render integration; ensure `RenderSnapshot` always provides `fields` for non-public renders, or extend the engine to warn when `html` is present but `fields` is absent on non-public viewers.** (`packages/contracts/src/public-pages/scrape.ts`)

The `0`-prefix variant also matches `0[6-9]\d{9}` formatted landlines. V1 limitation; spec documents patterns as "conservative". **Trigger: Story 2.5 / first real public HTML snapshot is wired into `loadSnapshots()`; refine regex and add context-aware exclusions (e.g., reject matches inside URL paths or quoted attributes) before live renders are scanned.** (`packages/contracts/src/public-pages/scrape.ts`) — See also Story 11a.4 obfuscation as defence-in-depth.

The gate will remain permanently green (no-op) on every PR regardless of what renders exist in CI until someone explicitly extends it. **Trigger: Story 2.5 (public Astro build HTML available in CI); add env-var-driven or convention-based snapshot discovery (e.g., `PUBLIC_SNAPSHOTS_DIR` or `apps/public/dist/snapshots/`).**  Note: the architecture-committed live-render path is `tests/integration/public-pages/scrape-test.spec.ts` (D13-1.2), which imports the engine separately — `loadSnapshots()` in the gate is the secondary path. (`packages/contracts/scripts/check-pii-scrape.ts`)

## Deferred from: code review of 2-1-i18n-centralized-utility-bilingual-surface-contract (2026-06-20)

- **CR-D1-2.1: React import at module top level contaminates server-safe callers** — `packages/i18n/src/locale.ts` imports React unconditionally; any `import` from `@twt/i18n` (including `getLocale`, `t`, numerals) drags React into the module graph. Explicitly acknowledged in spec + README as inert at v1 (no non-React server consumers yet). **Trigger: first non-React server consumer of `@twt/i18n` (e.g. `apps/api` for API copy/locale) — split React hooks into a `@twt/i18n/react` subpath export at that point.** (`packages/i18n/src/locale.ts:20`; `packages/i18n/README.md` §Server consumers)

- **CR-D2-2.1: Parity gate only checks en→hi direction; orphaned hi-only keys are silently ignored** — `scripts/lib.ts:checkParity` iterates `en/` namespaces and checks for missing Hindi entries but never flags Hindi keys that have no English counterpart. Beyond AC3 scope at v1 (no bi-directional requirement). **Trigger: first story that introduces manual hi/ edits outside the en/ → hi/ authoring flow — add a reverse-direction check to `checkParity` to catch stale Devanagari strings.** (`packages/i18n/scripts/lib.ts:39-61`)

- **CR-D3-2.1: `toGregorianNumeral` indexOf fragility for `[०-९]` range characters not in HINDI_DIGITS** — `HINDI_DIGITS.indexOf(d)` returns -1 for a character matched by the `[०-९]` regex but absent from the tuple, then `String(-1)` = `"-1"` corrupts output. Impossible in current Unicode (U+0966–U+096F maps exactly to HINDI_DIGITS 0–9); theoretical fragility only. **Fix when relevant:** `const idx = HINDI_DIGITS.indexOf(d as ...); return idx === -1 ? d : String(idx);` (`packages/i18n/src/number.ts:33`)

- **CR-D4-2.1: Module-level singleton in `classification.ts` creates test-ordering sensitivity** — `const registry = new Map(...)` is initialized once per Vitest worker; `declareSurface` mutations persist across tests in the same file. Current tests use unique namespace names and are safe; latent risk grows as the test suite expands. **Trigger: any new test file that calls `declareSurface` with a namespace already used elsewhere in the worker — add a `resetRegistryForTest()` export gated behind `process.env.NODE_ENV === 'test'` at that point.** (`packages/i18n/src/classification.ts:77-78`)

- **CR-D5-2.1: `useT()` creates a new closure on every render with no `useCallback` stabilization** — `useT()` returns a new anonymous function each render; components that receive it as a prop bypass `React.memo`. No correctness impact; performance concern that compounds with consumer count. **Trigger: first component passing the result of `useT()` as a prop to a memoized child — wrap in `useCallback` at that point.** (`packages/i18n/src/resolver.ts:82-84`)

- **CR-D6-2.1: No escape mechanism for literal `{word}` tokens in translation strings** — `TOKEN = /\{(\w+)\}/g` always interpolates; a translation value needing to display `{literal}` has no supported escape syntax. Not required by AC1 at v1 (no such use case in `common.json`). **Trigger: first translation string that needs a literal brace rendered verbatim — adopt an escape convention (e.g. `{{word}}` → `{word}`) at that point.** (`packages/i18n/src/resolver.ts:32`)

- **CR-D7-2.1: Gate teeth proven manually only; no automated subprocess test of `check-parity.ts` exit codes** — `tests/parity.test.ts` tests the pure `lib.ts` core comprehensively; the impure `check-parity.ts` script's exit-1-on-failure / exit-0-on-clean behavior was verified manually during development (en-only key → exit 1 → fixed → exit 0). The 1.17 precedent for subprocess testing is unclear from context. **Trigger: if a future code review of a comparable gate (e.g. the tokens `check-theme-determinism.ts`) reveals it has automated subprocess tests — retrofit `check-parity.ts` with the same pattern for parity.** (`packages/i18n/tests/`)

## Deferred from: code review of 3-2-member-mobile-otp-authentication (2026-06-25)

- **W1-3.2: Access token revocation list absent.** JWT access tokens (≤15 min) cannot be invalidated before expiry without a revocation blacklist. Spec-committed control is the short TTL; refresh-chain revocation stops future tokens but does not invalidate already-issued access tokens. **Re-trigger:** if a force-re-OTP or fraud signal (§2.2 line 1338) requires immediate access invalidation, a token blacklist keyed on `jti` or a short-TTL session-lookup endpoint will be needed. (`member-session-guard.ts`)

- **W2-3.2: Stale `phoneCounts` entries survive server clock jumps >1 window bucket.** The cleanup loop deletes only keys not ending in the current bucket suffix; a monotonic clock jump of >1 bucket leaves old entries until next roll. Acceptable limitation under normal conditions. **Re-trigger:** if NTP-synchronized deployments with clock-step events are encountered. (`otp-rate-limit.ts`)

- **W3-3.2: `selectPariwar` TOCTOU — `mobile_blind_index` in JWT could be stale if mobile changes between token issuance and call.** Structural limitation of the 5-min pariwar-select token: membership re-resolution at call time mitigates most attacks but not a same-window mobile-reassignment. Full closure requires a persistent nonce table matching the token. **Re-trigger:** when mobile-change flow (later epic) is built — ensure select-tokens are invalidated on mobile update. (`member-auth.handlers.ts:selectPariwar`)

## Deferred from: code review of 3-6a-member-creation-signup-continuation-wizard-assembly-tc-acceptance (2026-06-28)

- **D1 — `emitAuthAudit` synchronous throw after `ok = true` emits false compensating `member_terms.accept_rolled_back` while scope-tx commits.** (`apps/api/src/modules/terms/member-terms.handlers.ts`) If `auditSink.emit` throws synchronously, the catch block fires a `member_terms.accept_rolled_back` 5xx compensating line, then `finally` runs `closeScopeTx(ok=true)` — committing the scope-tx. The result is a committed consent row alongside a spurious "rolled_back" audit entry. This is a pre-existing pattern copied verbatim from the `medical.handlers.ts` P1 template, which has the same ordering (`ok = true` → synchronous `emitAuthAudit`). **Re-trigger:** any future audit-sink implementation that can throw synchronously; or when Epic 6 (`claim_time_dpdpa`) copies this chain and the ordering becomes load-bearing.

- **D2 — `consumeSignupContinuation` TOCTOU window: probe SELECT runs outside the UPDATE's lock; expired+consumed row yields 409 instead of 401.** (`apps/api/src/modules/auth/member/member-auth.repo.ts`) The UPDATE (which missed because the row was already consumed or expired) and the follow-up probe SELECT run as two separate statements on the pool with no wrapping BEGIN/COMMIT. The `consumed_at` transition is write-once (NULL → non-null, never back), so the race window is benign. An expired+consumed row returns 409 `auth.signup_continuation_consumed` when a strict AC1(c) interpretation would prefer 401 — both are equivalent to the caller after the 30-min TTL. **Re-trigger:** if the continuation table ever gains a revocation/reset path that changes `consumed_at` back to NULL.

- **D3 — After `completeMemberLogin` fails post-commit, member exists in `pending-kyc` with no session; re-attempt returns 409 `auth.member_already_exists`; error message gives no recovery path.** (`apps/api/src/modules/auth/member/signup.handlers.ts`) The scope-tx commits (member + identity row written) before `completeMemberLogin` is called (auth writes run on `deps.pool` post-commit per R1). If `completeMemberLogin` throws, the continuation is consumed and the duplicate guard blocks retry. The user can recover via the returning-member OTP path (`/otp/request` → `/otp/verify` → full session), but `t('signup.error_generic')` does not communicate this. **Re-trigger:** any future work that improves signup error recovery UX, or if `member_trusted_devices` INSERT failure is observed in production.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-8-annual-renewal-with-3-month-grace-vyawastha-shulk-status-payload-reminder-cadence (2026-06-30)

- **W1 — Reminder cadence has no catch-up for skipped cron days.** An exact-match `daysSince` check means any cron outage spanning an offset day (+30/+60/+75/+89) permanently drops that reminder for affected members. Re-trigger: if reminder delivery becomes SLA-critical or the cron reliability budget shrinks; fix = ±1 day window or a "sent-marker" table.
- **W2 — `getLatestReceipt` (paidAt DESC) vs `selectRenewalCandidates` (valid_through DESC) disagree on "latest" receipt.** Theoretical only — no current code path produces receipts where `paidAt` and `valid_through` are out-of-order. Re-trigger: any admin tool or variable-term receipt feature that can stamp `valid_through` independently of `paidAt`.
- **W3 — `renewIntent` accepts non-renewable states.** A `pending-fee` or `lock-in` member can obtain a valid UPI intent URL and complete a UPI payment that the system then rejects at `renewConfirm` (409 `not_renewable`). Consistent with the signup intent pattern; mobile UI self-suppresses the CTA for non-eligible members. Re-trigger: if renewIntent becomes API-accessible without the mobile UI gate.
- **W4 — `VyawasthaShulkConfirmRequest` reused for renewal confirm exposes `referenceCode` (signup-only field).** Clients can send `referenceCode` on a renewal confirm and receive 200; the field is silently ignored. Cosmetic; add a dedicated `VyawasthaShulkRenewalConfirmRequest` when the contracts layer is next touched.
- **W5 — Candidate scan accumulates all terminal-state members indefinitely.** `selectRenewalCandidates` has no lower-bound date filter or state exclusion, so `lapsed-unpaid`, `withdrawn`, and `anonymized` members with old receipts remain in the scan set forever. Correctness is preserved (scheduler emits nothing for those states). Add a state or date lower-bound filter before the lapsed population exceeds ~50 k.
- **W6 — `renewalStatus` returns a non-null payload for withdrawn members, rendering the renewal widget and a CTA that ends in silent 409.** Data integrity is preserved (`renewConfirm` rejects non-renewable states). UX degrades only for the withdrawn-with-recent-receipt edge population.
- **W7 — Stale `from_state` in `renewConfirm` event payload when grace-expiry cron races the confirm.** The `from_state` in the emitted `vyawastha_shulk_paid` payload may reflect the pre-read state rather than the actual state at emission time. Reducer ignores `from_state`; impact is audit log imprecision only. Acceptable under READ COMMITTED isolation.
- **W8 — `onRenew` swallows all errors silently (no toast/feedback on 503/401).** Intentional fail-soft per mobile spec for the home-screen widget. Revisit when Epic 5 adds a structured notification surface or a dedicated payment screen is introduced (see D1).

## Deferred from: implementation of 3-10-voluntary-withdrawal-flow-with-110-forfeit-12-month-rejoin-lock (2026-07-02)

- **W1 — Architecture §2.12 Aadhaar-HMAC rejoin key is UN-ATTESTED / not-yet-wired (OPEN RISK).** The epic AC ("rejoin under same `aadhaar + ehrms` is blocked") and architecture §2.12:1735 ("Aadhaar HMAC hash retained for the 12-month rejoin lock") CANNOT be implemented as written against the shipped code: the full Aadhaar is masked to last-4 at the KYC provider boundary (`digilocker/mapper.ts:4-5`) and discarded, eHRMS is stored nowhere, and manual-fallback KYC members have no Aadhaar at all. **v1 keys the 12-month rejoin lock on the mobile blind index** (`member_identities.mobile_blind_index`, the durable identity blind-index that already backs 3.6a signup dedup). This lock is **evadable with a fresh mobile number** — the architecture-committed Aadhaar-HMAC control is therefore NOT satisfied and is carried as open risk. A NULLABLE `member_withdrawals.aadhaar_hmac` forward-compat SEAM column ships now so **Story 3.3a can backfill the architecture-committed Aadhaar-HMAC key later WITHOUT a schema migration** (the table grants UPDATE for exactly this). Decision user-confirmed 2026-07-01. Re-trigger: Story 3.3a (when a durable Aadhaar-derived identifier exists to HMAC), or any KYC change that retains a stable Aadhaar derivation.
- **W2 — Post-12-month rejoin path (`withdrawn → pending-fee` reactivation) is OUT OF SCOPE for v1.** Architecture §1.14:1248 routes a re-signup after the lock period `withdrawn → pending-fee`, but that reactivation collides with the `member_identities` `UNIQUE(pariwar_id, mobile_blind_index)` row (the withdrawn member's identity row still exists) and is a distinct capability. For v1, a withdrawn member whose 12-month window has ELAPSED still receives the unchanged 409 `auth.member_already_exists` at signup (NOT a reactivation). The signup handler + `signup-create.spec.ts` explicitly assert this current behavior. Re-trigger: when a reactivation/re-signup capability is scheduled (needs a decision on identity-row reuse vs. mint-new + a `withdrawn → pending-fee` transition emitter).
- **W3 — Withdrawal confirm audit line omits masked mobile.** AC4 names "masked mobile / member_id / reason_code" for the `member_withdrawal.completed` audit context; the implementation carries `member_id` (via `actorId`) + `reason_code` + `rejoin_permitted_at`, but NOT masked mobile — obtaining it would require decrypting the Tier-1 mobile ciphertext solely for an audit line (the life-events audit precedent likewise does not decrypt the mobile). The `actorId` already identifies the member. The signup rejoin-block line (`member_withdrawal.rejoin_blocked`) DOES carry masked mobile (it has the plaintext in hand). Re-trigger: if audit consumers need masked-mobile on the withdrawal-completed line specifically.
- **W4 — No dedicated profile/settings screen for the withdrawal entry point.** The app has no profile/settings surface yet; the understated `WithdrawalEntry` is placed at the very bottom of the home tab (chromeless, muted, low-prominence — deliberately not a CTA). Re-trigger: when a profile/settings screen lands, move the entry there.
- **W5 — No rate limit / write-throttle on the withdrawal confirm route.** Pre-existing architectural gap shared with all member routes (see 3.9 Defer-1). The step-up gate + the withdrawable-state guard bound abuse in practice (a member can withdraw at most once). Re-trigger: if abuse vectors are observed or a global member-route throttle is introduced.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-10-voluntary-withdrawal-flow-with-110-forfeit-12-month-rejoin-lock (2026-07-02)

- **CR-W1 — Phantom audit on COMMIT failure (scope-tx design).** `emitAuthAudit` fires after `ok = true` but before `closeScopeTx` COMMIT in the finally block. If COMMIT fails, the audit record says "withdrawal completed" but no DB state change exists. Pre-existing pattern shared with all member handlers (nominee, life-events, etc.) — a scope-tx architectural limitation. Re-trigger: if the scope-tx module is refactored to expose commit errors to callers.
- **CR-W2 — `withdrawn_at` in `member_withdrawals` diverges slightly from `occurred_at` in `events_log`.** `withdrawnAt = deps.clock()` is captured before `openScopeTx`; `events_log.occurred_at` is set by DB `now()` at insert time. The two timestamps for the same conceptual event are always slightly different. Pre-existing clock/scope-tx architecture pattern. Re-trigger: if audit consumers need strict wall-clock parity between the withdrawal record and the event row.
- **CR-W3 — FOR ALL RLS policy scope allows any same-pariwar `twt_app` session to UPDATE any member's withdrawal row.** The `tenant_isolation_write` policy scopes on `pariwar_id` only, not `member_id`. By-design for the `aadhaar_hmac` seam backfill (Story 3.3a) + RTBF; no current route exposes UPDATE to a non-actor. Re-trigger: when Story 3.3a wires the Aadhaar-HMAC backfill or if a route is ever added that modifies withdrawal rows.
- **CR-W4 — `withdrawn` state + no `member_withdrawals` row → silent 409 trap at signup.** The LEFT JOIN on `member_withdrawals` in `resolveMembersByMobile` returns `null` for `rejoin_permitted_at` when the member's withdrawal row was deleted without updating `members.state` (e.g., an inconsistent admin action). The rejoin-lock check short-circuits to `auth.member_already_exists` — wrong code for a withdrawn member. Not reachable in normal operation (the scope-tx insert + FK cascade keep these in sync). Re-trigger: if a RTBF partial-delete path or admin tool can set `members.state = 'withdrawn'` without a corresponding `member_withdrawals` row.
- **CR-W5 — URL params in `rejoin-locked.tsx` not validated before date formatting.** `withdrawnDate`/`rejoinDate` come from `useLocalSearchParams` (URL-controllable). `formatWithdrawalDate` has a graceful fallback (`iso.slice(0, 10)`) but will render the first 10 chars of any injected string. Display-only, no security impact; requires deliberate deep-linking. Re-trigger: if the screen gains any action gated on these dates.
- **CR-W6 — Past-window withdrawn member loses forensic audit traceability at signup.** When `isTerminal && now >= rejoinPermittedAt`, the code emits `member_signup.failure` with `reason: 'member_already_exists'` — indistinguishable from a live-duplicate audit event. An auditor cannot identify past-window withdrawal attempts. Same category as W2 (post-window rejoin path deferred). Re-trigger: when W2 (post-window reactivation) is implemented.
- **CR-W7 — `resolveMembersByMobile` LEFT JOIN `member_withdrawals` missing `pariwar_id` predicate.** `LEFT JOIN member_withdrawals mw ON mw.member_id = mi.member_id` does not filter on `pariwar_id`. In the R2 multi-pariwar case, a withdrawal from Pariwar X would be joined to Pariwar Y's identity row, polluting the rejoin-lock check for Pariwar Y. Latent — R2 is explicitly D1 in v1 (single-pariwar). Fix: add `AND mw.pariwar_id = mi.pariwar_id` to the join. Re-trigger: when R2 multi-pariwar is implemented.
- **CR-W8 — Stale `now` in signup rejoin-lock boundary comparison.** `const now = deps.clock()` fires at the top of `signupCreate` before the jti-consume and `resolveMembersByMobile` round-trips. Under load, enough time can elapse that the boundary comparison is slightly stale. Impact: milliseconds against a 12-month window. Pre-existing pattern across the signup handler. Re-trigger: if sub-second precision on the boundary becomes a product requirement.

The single-active-export idempotency guard (AC1) bounds abuse (a member can have at most one in-flight export). Re-trigger: if abuse vectors are observed or a global member-route throttle is introduced.

- **CR-A-W3 — `getExportForMember` has no explicit `pariwarId` predicate.** RLS provides tenant isolation; the explicit pariwarId filter would be defense-in-depth only. Consistent with the project's per-table predicate pattern (`member_id` cross-tenant defense-in-depth, pariwar via RLS). Re-trigger: if a superuser/admin surface is added that bypasses RLS.

If this state arises, `findActiveExport` also excludes the row (`gt(expiresAt, now)` evaluates to NULL → no match), stranding the PII artifact at rest forever. Re-trigger: if the job UPDATE is refactored and `expiresAt` is accidentally dropped.
- **CR-A-W5 — `orderBy(sql\`${dataExports.createdAt} DESC\`)` in `findActiveExport` vs `orderBy(desc(...))` used elsewhere.** Cosmetic inconsistency; raw SQL template bypasses Drizzle's column type safety. No functional impact. Re-trigger: at next Drizzle upgrade or store.ts refactor.

- **CR-B-W1 — `DataExportStatusResponse` flat optionals instead of discriminated union.** `readyAt`/`expiresAt` are `.optional()` regardless of `status`; TypeScript won't enforce that `readyAt` exists when `status === 'ready'`. Spec defines a flat shape so intentional, but a discriminated union would give consumers stronger type guarantees. Re-trigger: when mobile/api-client hits a runtime undefined on `readyAt` in a ready-state handler.

- **CR-B-W4 — Bare `z.string()` for enum-like fields** (`consentType`, `actorRole`, `kyc.verificationStrength`, `kyc.source`, `member.state`). Named values enforced at domain/DB layer. Re-trigger: if a renamed enum value causes a silent mismatch in the ZIP output.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-11-data-export-zip-dpdpa-member-right Group C — Jobs (2026-07-02)

- **CR-C-W1 — No audit entry on export failure path.** The success path writes `member_data_export.generated`; the failure path writes nothing. For a DPDPA data-rights feature, every terminal state should be auditable. Re-trigger: if DPDPA regulatory review requires a failure audit trail, or if a member disputes a failed export.
- **CR-C-W3 — Cron validation regex accepts any 5-token string, not a valid cron expression.** `/^(\S+\s+){4}\S+$/` passes `abc xyz 99 foo bar`. pg-boss or the underlying scheduler will silently misparse or reject an invalid expression at runtime and the vacuum never fires. Re-trigger: use a proper cron-parser library (`cron-parser`, `cronstrue`) in the regex gate.
- **CR-C-W4 — Error messages contain literal `'...'` placeholders; `markErr` error object not included in alarm.** `throw new Error('[jobs:deps] KMS_TEST_MODE=live requires ADMIN_KEK_RESOURCE_NAME, ...')` does not name the missing variable; on-call cannot determine which env var is absent. The `markErr` alarm shows only a static string, not the caught error. Re-trigger: if a KMS boot failure or a mark-failed failure is ever reported without actionable diagnostics.
- **CR-C-W5 — No artifact size limit before ZIP → encrypt → store.** A member with a very large event stream could produce an arbitrarily large ZIP payload that blocks the worker thread, exhausts pool connections, and fills storage unchecked. Re-trigger: when Epic 7/8 contribution history lands and exports can grow to several MiB; add an `artifactBytes > MAX_BYTES` guard that flips the row to `failed` with `failed_reason = 'artifact_too_large'`.
- **CR-C-W6 — `encryptTier1` AAD omits `exportId`/`memberId`; within-pariwar ciphertext substitution is undetectable.** Two exports in the same pariwar with the same `fieldClass` have AAD-compatible ciphertexts: swapping them in the DB (accidental or malicious) produces a successful decryption returning the wrong member's data with no integrity error. A proper fix requires binding `exportId` to the AAD context passed to `encryptTier1`. Re-trigger: if multi-member pariwars make cross-member swaps a plausible threat model.
- **CR-C-W7 — pariwarId-missing path cannot update the DB row (architectural gap).** When `envelope.pariwarId` is absent the worker alarms and returns `failed` but the `data_exports` row stays `pending` forever (vacuum skips it; `findActiveExport` blocks re-requests). A proper fix requires either a BYPASSRLS service account or a security-definer function to update by `exportId` alone without a scope context. The enqueueing API always sets `pariwarId` from the authenticated session so this path is an invariant violation in practice. Re-trigger: if a missing-pariwarId alarm fires in production.

Any un-consumed export row still holds the member's full PII after RTBF; the existing `data_export.vacuum` cron zeroes `artifact_ciphertext` within 24h of expiry (Story 3.11). No explicit cleanup is added to the RTBF tx (adding a `data_exports` delete would break the soft-delete invariant). HONEST: there is a ≤24h window where a not-yet-vacuumed export artifact outlives the field-level anonymization. Re-trigger: if the residual-window is deemed a compliance gap, add an RTBF-time `artifact_ciphertext` zeroing of any live `data_exports` rows for the member.

## Deferred from: 3-12-rtbf-soft-delete-anonymization (scope decisions, 2026-07-03)

- **RTBF-D5 (compliance gap acknowledged) — `consent_records.consentPayload.ip` and `.userAgent` survive RTBF.** `ConsentPayload` includes `ip?: string` and `userAgent?: string` (consent_records.ts). Consent history rows are intentionally retained under the soft-delete model; these optional fields survive an RTBF anonymization. Under DPDPA's right to erasure, IP addresses may qualify as personal data. No erasure is applied at RTBF time. Re-trigger: legal/DPO review of whether IP and userAgent in a consent-record context constitute personal data under the trust's DPDPA posture; if yes, add targeted `consent_payload` JSON surgery (nulling `ip`/`userAgent`) inside `anonymizeMember`.

## Deferred from: code review of 4-1-rule-evaluation-engine-primitive (2026-07-03)

- **CR-4.1-D1 — Malformed per-operator arguments silently evaluate to `false` instead of `rule.payload_unrecognized`.** A condition like `{ op: 'fact_equals' }` (missing `fact`/`value`) passes `RuleConditionSchema` (which only validates `op: z.string().min(1)`) and reaches the operator; the operator reads `String(undefined)` as the fact name, finds no fact, and returns `passed=false`. The rule evaluates to `on_fail` rather than emitting `rule.payload_unrecognized`. The spec's "malformed payload" contract covers the outer envelope only; per-operator argument validation was not required. A misconfigured rule silently denies (the safer failure mode). Re-trigger: if a misconfigured rule produces unexplained denials and the subClauseResults are insufficient for diagnosis; or when per-operator Zod schemas are added in 4.2–4.5.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-12-rtbf-soft-delete-anonymization (2026-07-03)

- **CR-3.12-D1 — Audit-timing: phantom audit on COMMIT failure / 500 on sink throw.** `emitAuthAudit` fires inside the `try` block after `ok = true`, before `closeScopeTx` COMMIT runs in `finally`. Two low-probability failure modes: (a) if COMMIT throws, the audit record says "anonymized" while the DB rolled back to `withdrawn`; (b) if `auditSink.emit` throws synchronously, the RTBF commits (ok=true in finally) but the HTTP response is 500, leaving the client unable to confirm erasure. Both are pre-existing patterns mirroring the withdrawal handler. Re-trigger: if audit sink reliability or commit-failure observability becomes a concern, add a post-commit audit emit path (requires scope-tx API change).
- **CR-3.12-D2 — Non-locking `getMemberStateAt` in `assertAnonymizable` (no `SELECT FOR UPDATE`).** Two concurrent RTBF requests can both read `state = withdrawn`, both proceed through `anonymizeMember` (idempotent re-encryption), then race to `projectMemberState` where the second hits the `(stream_id, event_version)` unique constraint (23505) — correctly caught and mapped to 409. Design works as-is; `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the members row would eliminate the 23505-reliance entirely. Re-trigger: if the event-stream uniqueness constraint is relaxed or if `projectMemberState` ever gains a silent-conflict path.
- **CR-3.12-D3 — `anonymizeMember` WHERE clauses omit explicit `pariwarId` filter; tenant isolation relies solely on RLS.** The `pariwarId` param is used for encryption context only, not for row filtering. All other domain write accessors share this pattern (RLS-only trust model). Under a superuser or misconfigured pool connection, anonymizeMember would overwrite across all pariwars for the given memberId. Re-trigger: if the project adopts explicit pariwar predicates on domain write accessors.
- **CR-3.12-D4 — `confirm` handler hardcodes `state: 'anonymized'` before verifying `projectMemberState` return value.** The response is constructed with `state: 'anonymized'` before `projectMemberState` completes. If the projector succeeded silently without advancing state (a hypothetical path not possible given the pre-committed reducer), the response would claim anonymization that didn't occur. Pre-existing pattern (withdrawal handler). Re-trigger: if `projectMemberState` ever gains a non-throwing no-op path.

## Deferred from: code review of 4-2-r7-contribution-discipline-rules (2026-07-03)

- **CR-4.2-D1 — 7 uncached `resolveByClauseId` calls per re-eval in `evaluateR7LadderAt` (shell read amplification).** The shell resolves each clause payload a second time (after `evaluateAt`) to extract `precedence` data. This is a known design limitation: `evaluateAt` does not surface the resolved payload. On a cache-hit re-evaluation, `evaluateAt` returns instantly but the secondary resolves still fire (7 extra DB round-trips). Documented in `r7-ladder.ts` and accepted in the Dev Agent Record. Re-trigger: when Story 4.6 runs the 100×-thread gate and measures ladder latency under concurrent load; fix by exposing the resolved payload from `evaluateAt` or caching per-`at` in the shell.

- **CR-4.2-D2 — Shell `isApplied` compares `result.decision` against `meta.onPass` derived from a second independent `resolveByClauseId` call.** Both calls use the same pinned `at`, so the risk is near-zero with immutable clause rows. Theoretically, if two `clause_versions` rows exist with the same `effective_date` (concurrent seed-and-test scenario), the two resolves could pick different rows, causing a valid `applied` clause to be marked `applied: false`. Pre-existing by design; documented as "NOT a TOCTOU window." Re-trigger: if integration tests ever seed multiple versions with the same `effective_date`, or if clause mutability is introduced.

- **CR-4.2-D3 — R7(C) encodes a lighter lock-in than R7(F): 3 months vs 5 months + `complete_all`.** A member with a 12+ month gap (R7(C)) faces less institutional friction than one with a 6–11 month gap (R7(F)). This may be intentional ("treat as new registration" is a different path, not softer), but the asymmetry is not flagged in the seed comment. `policy_review_required: true` is set. Re-trigger: Trustee Panel review of FR-9 R7(C) vs R7(F) lock-in parameters; the seed comment should note the asymmetry explicitly.

- **CR-4.2-D4 — `selectDbNow` is exported from `evaluate.ts` but not from the barrel `index.ts`, creating an implicit internal API surface.** Any downstream importing directly from `evaluate.js` bypasses the barrel. Intentional per Dev Agent Record ("internal, not on the barrel"). Re-trigger: if a second consumer outside `r7-ladder.ts` needs `selectDbNow`, promote to a proper internal utility module.

- **CR-4.2-D5 — `fact_lt` admits `max: Infinity` / `max: NaN` (silent always-true/always-false).** `typeof Infinity === 'number'` is truthy; `actual < Infinity` is always true for any finite `actual`. Same gap exists in `fact_gte`. Accepted pattern from 4.1 per-operator arg-validation defer (see CR-4.1-D1). Re-trigger: when per-operator Zod schemas are added (4.3–4.5 extension); add `Number.isFinite(max)` guard to both `fact_lt` and `fact_gte` at that time.
