- **`lapseNetting` is threaded through the public API but never consulted in `deriveRetirementFacts`.** `packages/validity-service/src/producer.ts:65-70` — harmless while `LapseNettingPolicy` has one member (`'gross'`), but this is decorative plumbing today, not the "swap the enum, zero engine change" mechanism CR-4.6-D6 describes. **Re-trigger:** when the Trustee Panel resolves [[CR-4.5-D2]] and a second policy value is added — the producer will need an actual branch, not just a signature change.

- **`assertCanReadValidity` and `redactForCaller` are independently exported with no structural enforcement that both are always invoked together.** `packages/validity-service/src/redaction.ts` + `index.ts` — currently only `service.ts` calls them together; a future direct consumer could import one without the other and silently produce an insecure or over-redacted result. **Re-trigger:** if a second consumer of these exports is added outside `service.ts`.

- **The live-DB integration spec doesn't clean up the `audit_log_entries` rows it creates, unlike every other table it seeds.** `packages/validity-service/tests/integration/validity-service.spec.ts` — `clause_versions`/`member_postings`/`members`/`events_log`/`idempotency_keys` are all deleted in `afterAll`; `audit_log_entries` rows accumulate. Not a correctness issue (assertions key on membership/delta, not global counts), just an inconsistency against sibling cleanup in the same spec.

- **Nearly all of this package's DB-backed correctness guarantees only run when `DATABASE_URL` is set (`describe.skipIf`).** Compounded by the documented GitHub Actions suspension ([[project_ci_actions_suspension_local_mirror]]), this is a pre-existing repo-wide condition, not unique to this diff — noted so it isn't mistaken for a gap introduced by Story 4.6.

- **CR-4.6-D9 — the P0 100×-thread determinism gate only exercises synthetic fixtures, not the real producer/engine path (BigDev-confirmed deferral).** `packages/validity-service/tests/determinism-runner.ts` feeds hand-built `EvaluationResult` fixtures into `evaluateOrderedClauses`/`assemblePayload` — it never calls `deriveRetirementFacts` or `evaluateRetirementCoverageAt`. The gate genuinely proves the `Promise.all`/index-preserving-slot composition mechanism is race-safe, but a nondeterminism bug introduced in the real DB-backed producer or the engine call itself would not be caught by it, despite AC2's "any variance FAILS CI as a P0 architectural violation" billing. Expanding the harness to run DB-backed real data through the 100×-thread gate is substantial work, better scoped alongside Story 4.8. **Re-trigger:** Story 4.8, or sooner if a determinism incident is ever traced to the producer/engine layer rather than the composition layer.

- **CR-4.6-D10 — PRD's `medical_disclosure.declared_illnesses[]` is absent from the canonical payload (BigDev-confirmed deferral).** `MedicalDisclosureFlagsPayload` (`packages/validity-service/src/types.ts:83-91`) carries only `hasDisclosureOnRecord`/`declaredConditionCount`/`imaListVersion`/`pendingConcealmentFlag` — no field for the actual list of declared conditions. CR-4.6-D4 explains why the concealment *flag* can't decrypt-compare (Tier-1 encryption), but that rationale doesn't cover this broader omission. No current consumer needs the full list (Story 4.7's `<MemberStatusPanel>` isn't built yet). **Re-trigger:** if Story 4.7 or any Epic 6/10 admin/verifier view needs to render declared conditions (not just a count) — will need a decryption-capable seam similar to `concealmentAssessment`.

## Deferred from: Story 4.7 (`<MemberStatusPanel>` + AR-65 compound read model)

- **CR-4.7-D1 — the contribution read-model section stays `producer_unavailable` until the Epic 8/9 contribution producer lands.** `member_search_projection.contribution_section` + the panel's contribution-discipline section (d) carry the typed `{status:'producer_unavailable', producer:'epic-8-9'}` sentinel (D2-A), NEVER an empty array ([[project_engine_never_infers_contribution_facts]]). **Re-trigger:** when the Epic 8/9 contribution producer supplies real `contribution.*` events — the projector refresh (`member/search-projection.ts`) fills the section with real data (a producer-side change; zero panel/presenter change beyond rendering the populated list).

- **CR-4.7-D2 — the claim read-model section stays `producer_unavailable` until the Epic 6 claim producer lands.** `member_search_projection.claim_section` + the panel carry `{status:'producer_unavailable', producer:'epic-6'}` (D2-A), never `[]`. **Re-trigger:** Epic 6 claim filing — the projector fills the section with real claim events.

- **CR-4.7-D3 — name + Aadhaar member-search are DEFERRED (variance recorded against AC1).** AC1 enumerates search "by name, mobile, Aadhaar masked, Pariwar ID, etc." v1 ships mobile (blind index) + `member_id` + `pariwar_id` ONLY, exact-match (D3-A). Name + Aadhaar-as-search-key require deterministic blind indexes + schema migration + backfill — out of scope by design. "Aadhaar masked" is interpreted as a DISPLAY field (the panel identity section), not a v1 search key (D3 refinement v). **Re-trigger:** a dedicated "name/Aadhaar member-search blind index + migration + backfill" story (a separate migration story family; blind index is exact-match only by construction — prefix/fuzzy needs a different substrate).

- **CR-4.7-D4 — the human-readable membership-number identity feature (BigDev RE-CONFIRMED NOT 4.7 scope).** A membership number IS a real product requirement but does NOT exist in the substrate (`members` carries only `member_id` uuid + `pariwar_id`); the story must not invent a `member_number` column/search key. **Re-trigger:** the dedicated membership-number identity feature (schema + generation + backfill + indexing) — membership-number SEARCH lands alongside it, riding the same migration path as CR-4.7-D3.

- **CR-4.7-D5 — admin override / eligibility-override controls are OUT OF SCOPE (a WRITE surface).** UX mentions "admin-facing … with override controls; Trustee Panel review on overrides" (`ux-design-specification.md:1895`), but the epic AC is read + search + render only. No override write path was built. **Re-trigger:** a dedicated eligibility-override story (a write/eligibility-mutation surface with its own Trustee-Panel-review workflow).

- **CR-4.7-D6 — the 200ms validity cache / per-cohort invalidation is Story 4.8, not this story.** This story's `member_search_projection` is the admin member-SEARCH compound read model (~5s@4L, incremental-projector-maintained); the FR-12A validity payload's 200ms@4L cache is Story 4.8's Postgres materialized view (`architecture.md:1044-1048`) — a DISTINCT cache. **Re-trigger:** Story 4.8 (per-cohort cache invalidation with conservative-recompute fallback — the very next story).

- **CR-4.7-D7 — the admin (apps/admin) + member (apps/mobile) render variants + the p95/a11y test surfaces are NOT YET BUILT in this pass.** The backend + shared-logic vertical is complete and tested (the AR-65 read model + projector-exclusive write + trigger + CI gate; the exact-match search accessor; the strictly-pure `@twt/ui` presenter with 14 unit tests; the `@twt/contracts` wire DTOs; the apps/api `member-validity/` module — member-self + admin reads + search, typechecked). Still outstanding: the apps/admin `<MemberLookupForm>` + `<MemberStatusPanel>` (Radix/Tailwind) consuming the presenter via the TanStack-Query seam with a virtualized results list (Task 5); the apps/mobile `<MemberStatusPanel>` member variant (Tamagui) + its Home-reachable route + the Hindi-first `@twt/i18n` `member-status` locale keys + accessibility wiring (Task 6); the apps/api live-DB integration spec asserting admin-read-is-audited vs self-read-is-not + the ~5s@4L p95 harness + the compound-view a11y CI scan (Task 7 remainder). **Re-trigger:** the render-layer + full-integration continuation of Story 4.7 (the pure presenter + the wire DTOs + the api boundary make the two render layers a thin, drift-free consumption of a single tested view-model).

### Code review of Story 4.7 backend vertical (Fable, 2026-07-04) — dispositions

FIXED in-branch (4): (1) **no backfill** — migration 0035 now backfills `member_search_projection` from all existing `members` before RLS+trigger install (verified: populated 2231 pre-existing members on the dev DB); (2) **admin search un-audited** — `adminMemberSearch` now writes one `member.search` hash-chain audit line (actor + hashed criteria + result count) through the servicePool, per D5-A; (3) **fabricated 200 for nonexistent/cross-tenant member** — `adminValidityRead` now `memberExists`-probes and 404s before calling the service (no phantom payload / phantom audit); (6) **anonymized garbage masked mobile** — the search handler now suppresses BOTH `maskedMobile` and `name` for `state==='anonymized'`.

Deferred (recorded, NOT fixed):

- **CR-4.7-D8 — the presenter's headline derivation cannot distinguish two member populations from the validity payload alone (payload carries no lifecycle-state enum).** `packages/ui/src/member-status/presenter.ts:deriveHeadlineState` — (a) a paid, covered, IN-LOCK-IN member (`isValid=true, isActive=false, not-in-grace`) falls to `'pending-onboarding'`; (b) a WITHDRAWN member with payment history maps to `'expired-renewable'` + appeal-CTA, but withdrawal is a 12-month rejoin lock, not renewable. Root cause: `MemberValidityPayload` exposes `isValid/isActive/lockInStatus/vyawasthaShulk` but not the raw `members.state`. **Re-trigger:** the render-layer continuation (Tasks 5/6) — resolve by EITHER passing the lifecycle state into the presenter via `opts` (the admin variant already has it on the `member_search_projection` row; the member-self variant would need it added to the payload — which would be a `@twt/validity-service` payload change, flag to BigDev per the story's "if rendering forces a payload change, surface it" rule) OR a UX decision that blesses the current 5-state mapping. Add presenter tests for the valid-in-lock-in + withdrawn headline cases (the current 14 cover neither).

- **CR-4.7-D9 — `refreshMemberSearchProjection`'s `finally { SET LOCAL … 'off' }` can mask the real error with a 25P02.** `packages/domain/src/member/search-projection.ts:108-111` — if the upsert throws (FK / RLS WITH CHECK / trigger P0001), the tx aborts and the `finally`'s `client.query` fails with 25P02 ("current transaction is aborted"), replacing the original error. Every member-state write now routes through this. Precedent-consistent with `project.ts:138-140` (same pattern), so not a regression — but this diff doubles the exposure. **Re-trigger:** if a projection-refresh failure is ever debugged and the opaque 25P02 obscures the cause — guard the reset `SET LOCAL` in its own try/catch (or drop it, relying on the tx-scoped reset).

- **CR-4.7-D10 — the member-state-invariant static gate has known bypasses (the DB trigger is the real backstop).** `scripts/member-state-invariant/lib.ts:scanMemberSearchProjectionWrites` — matches only a bare `memberSearchProjection` identifier as the first arg of `.insert`/`.update`, only under `packages/domain/src`; `db.insert(schema.memberSearchProjection)`, aliasing, raw SQL, `.delete()`, or a writer under `apps/*`/`packages/jobs` all escape it. Identical known-shape limitation to the original `members.state` gate; the migration-0035 write-rejection trigger catches all of these at runtime regardless. **Re-trigger:** if a real out-of-band writer is ever added via one of the un-scanned forms (the trigger will fail it at runtime first).

- **CR-4.7-D11 — admin member-search results are NOT yet virtualized.** `apps/admin/src/modules/member-status/MemberSearchResults.tsx` renders a plain `overflow-y-auto` scroll region. Results are server-clamped (`limit ≤ 200`), so this is fine at v1 scale, but architecture (:2666-2678) names "Admin member-search results" a virtualized surface. Wiring TanStack Virtual / react-virtuoso adds a new UI dependency (out of the current change's scope). **Re-trigger:** when result pages grow past the clamp or a virtualization dependency is approved — swap the list body for a virtualized row renderer (the row component + selection contract already isolate this).

- **CR-4.7-D12 — no automated a11y CI scan for the compound views (harness absent).** Architecture (:2771-2775) calls for an automated a11y scan on compound views (AC3 automation). The repo has NO axe/a11y-scan harness today, and `apps/mobile`'s `test` script is a no-op (`true`) — there is no RN component-test runner. So the `<MemberStatusPanel>` accessibility is asserted structurally (semantic roles/labels/live-regions in the admin RTL test + the RN screen's `accessibilityRole`/`accessibilityLabel`/`accessibilityLiveRegion` props) but NOT by an automated scan. **Re-trigger:** when an axe-core / a11y-scan harness is introduced (a platform task) — wire the admin `<MemberStatusPanel>` + the mobile membership screen into it; add an RN test runner for the mobile app.

### Code review of story-4-7-member-status-panel-admin-member-facing-variants-compound-read-model (2026-07-04) — dispositions

Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor, diff `1d62ed4`..HEAD. Three findings (`deriveHeadlineState` mislabeling, the `finally SET LOCAL` error-masking, and the static write-gate bypasses) restated exactly what this file already records as **CR-4.7-D8/D9/D10** — not duplicated below. Three new gaps recorded:

- **CR-4.7-D13 — `redactionApplied` on the shared view-model mirrors `variant === 'member'`, not an actual payload-derived redaction signal.** `packages/ui/src/member-status/presenter.ts:284` — `MemberValidityPayloadDto` carries no field the presenter could read to determine whether a given payload was ACTUALLY redacted (vs. simply rendered in the member variant); the field is currently unused by both render layers (only `identitySuppressed` is consumed — confirmed via repo-wide grep). Exported as public shared-infra API on `MemberStatusViewModel`, so a narrower-scoped admin caller who legitimately receives a server-side-redacted payload would still see `redactionApplied: false`. **Re-trigger:** when a narrower admin scope with per-field server-side redaction ships (or anything starts consuming `redactionApplied`) — plumb a real signal from `@twt/validity-service` into the payload, or rename the field to reflect that it is variant-derived by design.

- **CR-4.7-D14 — the AR-65 admin-search p95 bench excludes the per-row Tier-1 KMS decryption cost.** `packages/domain/tests/bench/member-search-p95-budget.md` measures `searchMembers` (the DB query) alone; `adminMemberSearch` (`apps/api/src/modules/member-validity/handlers.ts`) additionally runs `decryptMobile`/`decryptKycField` (KMS-backed `decryptTier1` calls) over every result row via `Promise.all` — a cost with zero coverage in the recorded budget. The query-shape-only measurement itself follows the established CR-4.6-D2 measure-and-record precedent (full 4L seeding is impractical in CI); the decryption-cost exclusion is the new part. **Re-trigger:** the pre-launch ~4L scale-out validation harness (same trigger as CR-4.7-D6/CR-4.6-D2) — include the per-row decrypt cost in whatever harness ultimately validates the ~5s@4L admin-search budget end-to-end.

- **CR-4.7-D15 — `projectMemberState` now refreshes the AR-65 projection in the SAME transaction as every member-lifecycle write; if migration 0035 lags a deployed instance, every member-state write fails hard.** `packages/domain/src/member/project.ts:142-151` calls `refreshMemberSearchProjection` unconditionally after the state-cache write. This is a standing deploy-ordering assumption already inherent to this codebase's migration-coupled projector design (not unique to this diff — the state-cache write itself has the same coupling to its own migrations), so it is not a regression, but it is worth naming explicitly now that a second migration-coupled write shares the transaction. **Re-trigger:** if a rolling/blue-green deploy path is ever introduced that can run new application code against a not-yet-migrated schema (today's deploy discipline runs migrations before app rollout, so this is currently theoretical).

## Deferred from: code review of 4-8-per-cohort-cache-invalidation-with-conservative-recompute-fallback (2026-07-04)

- **`adminInvalidateValidityCache`'s epoch bump and its audit write are not atomic with each other.** `apps/api/src/modules/member-validity/handlers.ts:125-155` — the cohort-epoch bump runs on `scopeTx.tx` (the per-request scoped transaction), while the emergency-invalidation audit line is written via `deps.servicePool` (a separate BYPASSRLS connection). If the outer scoped transaction were ever rolled back after this handler returns, the hash-chain audit log would permanently assert an invalidation that never took effect. This is a pre-existing, systemic pattern — the identical split (mutating write on the scoped tx, `audit.writeAuditEntry` on `deps.servicePool`) is already used identically in `apps/api/src/modules/terms/member-terms.handlers.ts`, `apps/api/src/modules/terms-and-conditions/index.ts`, `apps/api/src/modules/medical/medical.handlers.ts`, and `apps/api/src/modules/rules/index.ts` — not introduced by this story. **Re-trigger:** if this class of audit/write non-atomicity is ever addressed system-wide (e.g. routing audit writes through the same scoped tx, or adding a reconciliation sweep), apply the same fix here.

## Deferred from: code review of 5-1-structured-alert-payload-schema-channel-provider-abstraction-central-dispatcher (2026-07-05)

- **Per-channel escaping semantics.** `packages/channels/src/render.ts:31` — 5.1's uniform `escapeText` (HTML-entity encode + backslash-escape markdown/template) keeps injection payloads inert on every channel, but escape syntax is channel-specific: ordinary admin prose (`Terms & Conditions (6 PM) #5`) reaches SMS/push/WA garbled (`&amp;`, `\(`, `\#`), and Telegram MarkdownV2-reserved chars (`! . - + = |`) are NOT escaped, so a real `parse_mode: MarkdownV2` send would 400. **Deferral rationale (decision 2026-07-05):** channel-correct escaping belongs where each real provider's parse mode is known; doing it in 5.1 would churn the determinism fixtures twice. **Re-trigger:** each provider story (5.2 FCM/APNs, 5.3, 5.4 WA, 5.5 Telegram, 5.6 SMS) MUST own its channel's escaping semantics as part of real-SDK integration — carry this as an explicit AC in each.

## Deferred from: code review of ai-5-1-access-wrapper-gate-extension (2026-07-08)

- **Switch/case equality bypasses the scanner.** `scripts/access-wrapper-invariants/lib.ts` (`unsafeCompareNode`) only matches `BinaryExpression`/`CallExpression` shapes; a verification context written as `switch (token) { case expectedToken: ... }` produces a `SwitchStatement`/`CaseClause` with no AST match anywhere in the scanner, so a Story-5.4-shaped defect rewritten as a switch passes clean. Secret verification is not expected to use switch/case; supporting it widens the heuristic for a pattern with no current usage. **Re-trigger:** if a real credential-verification implementation adopts this shape.
- **Name-only matching has no import resolution.** `calleeName` in `scripts/access-wrapper-invariants/lib.ts` matches purely on identifier text at the call site — a locally shadowed/mocked function named `timingSafeEqualString`/`resolveChannelSecret`/`createHmac` satisfies verification/approved-comparator checks regardless of its real implementation, while an aliased import (`import { timingSafeEqual as tse }`) is invisible to the approved-comparator check. Proper import/symbol resolution requires semantic analysis rather than a cheap AST heuristic, exceeding AI-5-1's intended "cheapest mechanizable slice." **Re-trigger:** if aliasing causes a real false negative or false positive.
- **Verification signal and compare split across nested scopes evades detection.** `collectOwnNodes` (`scripts/access-wrapper-invariants/lib.ts`) deliberately stops at each nested function-like boundary and `isVerificationContext` is evaluated independently per scope. Today's `handlers.ts` methods (`verifyChallenge`/`receive`/`receiveTelegram`) each independently call `resolveChannelSecret`, so they self-qualify — but a refactor that resolves the secret once in the outer factory and only compares it in an inner callback/method (the exact object-literal-methods-in-a-factory shape this code already uses) would silently defeat the gate. Detecting verification state across nested scopes requires control/data-flow analysis, moving beyond the project's lightweight invariant-gate philosophy. **Re-trigger:** if future refactoring introduces this pattern in production code.

The "exactly two, always together" invariant is enforced ONLY by `recordClaimNomineeBankAccounts` (`NomineeBankAccountSetError`, the sole writer) + the Zod `.length(2)` contract layer — confirmed via grep that no other code path writes to `claim_nominee_bank_accounts` today. Deliberately NOT closed with an improvised trigger/exclusion-constraint during this review — that needs its own design pass weighing write-path performance and RLS interaction, not a bolt-on during an unrelated fix cycle. **Re-trigger:** if a second write path to this table is ever introduced (a migration/backfill script, a different service), or if defense-in-depth against a direct-SQL mistake becomes a priority — design a focused DB-level cardinality constraint (e.g. a deferred `CONSTRAINT TRIGGER` asserting `count(*) per claim_case_id ∈ {0,2}` at statement end) as its own change. [packages/domain/src/claim/nominee-bank-persist.ts]

- **Tier-1 envelope-encryption AAD never binds ciphertext to row identity — the `EncryptionContext.rowKey` seam is unused system-wide, not a 6.8-specific gap.** `packages/domain/src/encryption/kms-provider.ts`'s `EncryptionContext` has an optional `rowKey` field for exactly this purpose, but a repo-wide grep shows NO production caller ever sets it — `nominee-bank-crypto.ts` (Story 6.8) and its stated precedent `ground-inspection-crypto.ts` (Story 6.7) both build AAD from only `{pariwarId, fieldClass}`. Practical consequence: ciphertext for account #1 and #2 of the SAME claim (and across DIFFERENT claims in the same Pariwar+field-class) share identical AAD — a ciphertext swap between rows (a data-migration mistake, a future buggy write path, direct DB tampering) would decrypt "successfully" with wrong plaintext; AEAD wouldn't catch it. No code path today can cause such a swap (each `prepareAccount()` call closure-captures its own entry/rank correctly), so this is a latent hardening gap, not an active bug. Fixing it for nominee-bank alone would break consistency with its own "verbatim copy of ground-inspection-crypto" precedent. **Compatibility strategy:** adding `rowKey` changes the AAD, so existing ciphertext (encrypted under the old context) would fail to decrypt if added unconditionally — needs either (a) a versioned re-encryption migration (decrypt under the old context, re-encrypt under the new one, tracked via the existing `enc:v1:`/potential `enc:v2:` envelope-version prefix, which `envelope.ts` already reserves for this kind of migration), or (b) a decrypt path that tries the new context and falls back to the old one until backfill completes. **Re-trigger:** a dedicated cross-cutting encryption-hardening story threading `rowKey` (e.g. `` `${claimCaseId}:${accountRank}` `` / `` `${tableName}:${primaryKey}` ``) through EVERY Tier-1 caller (nominee-bank, ground-inspection, claim-documents, member PII, etc.) together with a compatible migration plan — not a nominee-bank-only patch. [packages/domain/src/encryption/kms-provider.ts, apps/api/src/modules/claims/nominee-bank-crypto.ts, apps/api/src/modules/claims/ground-inspection-crypto.ts]

- **The shared error-mapping middleware forwards raw Zod validation issues verbatim to the client on every 400 — a system-wide footgun, not 6.8-specific.** `apps/api/src/middleware/error-mapping/index.ts:82-90` sends `issues: error.validation` straight into the JSON response body; `fastify-type-provider-zod`'s `createValidationError` embeds the FULL Zod issue object at `params.issue` for every validation failure across EVERY route in the API, not just nominee-bank. Confirmed no nominee-bank validator's issue shape embeds a raw submitted value today (Zod's regex/length/strict/custom-refine issues don't echo the input by default), so nothing leaks currently — but it's a footgun: any future `.refine()`/`.superRefine()` anywhere in `packages/contracts` whose message or `ctx.addIssue` interpolates the submitted value would leak it directly in a 400 body, invisibly to whoever reviews just that one contract file. **Re-trigger:** a dedicated hardening pass on `errorMappingHandler`'s Zod-validation branch — e.g. project `error.validation` down to `{keyword, instancePath, message}` only (dropping `params.issue`) before sending, or add a lint/test rule scanning `packages/contracts` for `.refine()`/`.superRefine()` calls that interpolate a parameter into their message. Fix once for the whole error-mapping boundary, not per-contract-file. [apps/api/src/middleware/error-mapping/index.ts]

## Deferred from: code review of ai-7-1-cycle-spawn-worker-orchestration-tests (2026-07-18)

- **Missing-`pariwarId` guard uncovered in both parent and child** — `runCycleSpawnParent` (cycle-spawn.ts:102-105) and `runCycleSpawnChild` (:200-203) both early-alarm-and-throw on a falsy `pariwarId`, and neither guard has a covering test. A regression dropping the guard (letting a null-tenant job proceed into `withPariwarScope`) would ship green. Outside the story's frozen 7-scenario matrix (Acceptance Auditor: "correctly absent"). **Re-trigger:** a dedicated tenant-boundary hardening pass on the jobs workers, or if either guard is ever refactored.
- **Child spawn-FAILURE `recordAborted` path uncovered** — the spawn `catch` (cycle-spawn.ts:235-238) records the `cycle.spawn.aborted` breadcrumb then rethrows; only the *finalize*-failure branch (which shares the same `recordAborted` helper) is tested. Design Notes explicitly call the spawn-failure variant "optional parity coverage, not required by this story's scope." **Re-trigger:** if the spawn and finalize failure paths ever diverge in what they record.
- **Zero-children plan uncovered** — an empty `frozenClaims`/`planResult.children` array drives the fan-out `for` loop zero times (`poolCount:0`, `boss.send` never called, `planned:true`), and this degenerate case is never asserted. Not in the matrix. **Re-trigger:** if a valid cycle-freeze can ever produce zero pools.
- **`childConcurrency = 0` `??`-vs-`||` boundary uncovered** — cycle-spawn.ts:283 uses `deps.childConcurrency ?? DEFAULT_CHILD_LOCAL_CONCURRENCY`; tests pass `20` and omit. Zero is the one value distinguishing `??` (keeps 0) from `||` (forces 8), so a refactor to `||` — silently forcing 8 workers when an operator set concurrency to 0 to quiesce the queue — is uncaught. Deferred per scope decision (option 1). **Re-trigger:** if a "pause this queue via concurrency 0" operation is ever added.
- **`parentIdempotencyTtlSeconds` wiring never asserted** — no test provides a custom TTL or asserts `store.claim` was called with `(key, ttl)` (cycle-spawn.ts:108,116). A bug wiring the wrong TTL risks a claim expiring mid-run and a concurrent retry re-allocating identifiers — the exact hazard the doc comment warns about. Deferred per scope decision (option 1). **Re-trigger:** if the TTL default or its source config changes.
- **non-`Error` thrown value → `String(err)` reason branch uncovered** — both failure tests throw `new Error(...)`, exercising only `err.message`; the `err instanceof Error ? … : String(err)` fallback (cycle-spawn.ts:212) is untested. Deferred per scope decision (option 1). **Re-trigger:** if a domain fn can throw a non-Error reject value.
- **`recordAborted` never-masks-original-error invariant uncovered** — `appendCycleAbortedMock` always resolves, so the documented "breadcrumb-append failure only alarms, never replaces the real error" catch (cycle-spawn.ts:221-225) is untested. Deferred per scope decision (option 1). **Re-trigger:** if the breadcrumb-recording error handling is ever refactored.
- **actorId/traceId propagation onto child envelopes unasserted** — the success-path test checks `payload` + `singletonKey` via `objectContaining` but never that `requestId`/`actorId`/`traceId` are threaded from the parent envelope onto each child envelope (cycle-spawn.ts:171-176). Dropped trace/actor context is uncaught. Deferred per scope decision (option 1). **Re-trigger:** as part of a trace-context propagation audit across the jobs workers.

## Deferred from: dev-story 7-4 (Deterministic Member-to-Pool Assignment) — D2 scope fork resolved to (B) (2026-07-18)

- **✅ DISCHARGED by AI-7-2 (2026-07-19)** — the live freeze-time assignable-roster query is now wired. `apps/jobs/src/assignable-roster.ts` (`createAssignableRosterResolver`) enumerates the Pariwar's members (`member.listMemberIdsForPariwar`, the new bulk read), evaluates each via `getValidityAt(..., committed_at, { internal: true })`, and keeps the `is_valid` set (D1 predicate `isMemberAssignable`); `runCycleSpawnChild` resolves it per-child (D2) and threads the `memberSet` into `spawnChildPool`, which flips `assignment_roster_wired → true`. Proven end-to-end on :5433 (`apps/jobs/tests/assignable-roster-live.test.ts`: real members + real validity + spawn → `resolveAssignedPoolForMember` returns the engine-placed pool). The `getValidityCached` note in the follow-up shape was superseded — that path is live-`now()`-only and never caches historical reads, so it would break the "NEVER now()" invariant; the O(N·M) warming comes from the engine's per-clause keyed-store memo instead. Original deferral below (kept for provenance):
- **The live freeze-time assignable-roster QUERY is deferred to a scoped follow-up (the D2 (A)→(B) fallback, BigDev pre-authorized).** Story 7.4's named deliverable — the version-pinned assignment ALGORITHM + balancing + roster fingerprint + property/replay/frozen-vector suite — shipped in full, and the REAL seam (`createPoolAssignmentSeam`) is wired into `apps/jobs/src/boot.ts` behind the stable `PoolAssignmentSeam` type. What is NOT wired is the production `memberSet` supply: `spawnChildPool` (`packages/domain/src/pool/spawn.ts:357-365`) still passes an empty `memberSet: []`, so the real seam returns `[]` and spawned pools carry no member assignments yet (the `member_state_hash` audit field is the empty-roster fingerprint `sha256("[]")`). **Why (A) full-wire was intractable within this story (all three are hard blockers, not effort):** (1) `@twt/domain` **cannot** import `@twt/validity-service` — the layering is validity-service → domain (verified: validity-service/package.json depends on @twt/domain), so the verdict-filtered roster query cannot live in the domain `spawnChildPool`; (2) there is **no bulk member-enumeration query** in the codebase (only per-member `getMemberStateAt` + the per-member validity cache), so a `listAssignableMembersAtFreeze(pariwarId, cycleId)` set-level read must be built from scratch; (3) D4 forbids `assign.ts` from encoding member-state policy — the assignable set must come from Validity verdicts — but the **validity-payload → "assignable" mapping is itself unresolved** (e.g. is `active-in-grace` assignable? the epic's excluded-state list is a *description* of the verdict, not a spec), a policy the follow-up must settle. **Shape of the follow-up:** build the set-level assignable-roster read (enumerate the pariwar's members, evaluate each against the Story 4.6 Validity Service at `cycle_freeze_commits.committed_at`, keep the assignable set) in a package that MAY import `@twt/validity-service` (i.e. `apps/jobs`, not domain), inject it into `runCycleSpawnChild` and thread the resulting `memberSet` into `spawnChildPool` (a new roster-supplier param) — the seam type + algorithm + `member_state_hash` code path are already in place, so this is purely the roster supply. Note the O(N·M) per-child validity recompute (each of N children re-evaluates M members) that Story 7.9 must validate against the <60s p95 envelope. **Re-trigger:** Story 7.6 (pool-bound payment) needs real assignments to resolve a member's VPA — it is the natural forcing function; wire the roster then (or as a dedicated `AI-7-x` follow-up before 7.6). [packages/domain/src/pool/spawn.ts:357-365, apps/jobs/src/cycle-spawn.ts, apps/jobs/src/boot.ts]

## Deferred from: implementation of 8-8-contribution-loop-notification-triggers (2026-07-23)

_Story 8.8 is the stack's FIRST live `dispatch()` call site. These four are deliberate, disclosed v1
scope boundaries — each was examined during implementation and consciously NOT built silently into the
fan-out. They are recorded here so the choices are visible rather than inferred from the code._

- **The AR-19 PER-RUNG durable retry adapter is not built (D9, the disclosed deviation).** AR-19 reads "push (1 + up to 3 backoff-spaced retries) → WA → SMS", i.e. retries happen per RUNG before advancing. Story 8.8 retries the WHOLE LADDER instead: `runChannelCascade` runs with `backoffMs: []` (one attempt per rung, no in-process sleeping — at 4L members the default 30s/5m/30m schedule would hold a worker 35+ minutes per undelivered member) and retry-with-backoff is delegated to pg-boss's own job retry. Two consequences, both examined and accepted: (a) a genuinely dead push reaches SMS FASTER, which is better for a time-critical cycle-open; (b) a retry pass cannot duplicate a paid send, because any rung that had succeeded would have stopped the ladder — a re-run only re-attempts rungs that already failed. What is LOST is the "give push three more chances before spending money on WA" bias. The refinement is a durable per-rung adapter: a job carrying `{ channel, attempt }` that re-enqueues itself with `startAfter`. **Re-trigger:** if production shows a meaningful volume of push-recoverable members being escalated to a paid channel too eagerly (i.e. the paid-send rate is materially higher than a per-rung ladder would produce). [apps/jobs/src/scheduler/contribution-notify.ts]
- **REAL per-Pariwar provider wiring for `apps/jobs`** — **✅ CLOSED by AI-8-3 (2026-07-25; artifact produced).** `boot.ts` now wires a real `resolveProviders` (`buildContributionProviderResolver`), so a deployed worker with the channel secrets/config provisioned delivers REAL bytes (real push as the cascade's first rung, real WA/SMS/Telegram per per-Pariwar config), and falls back to the log-only fixture per channel only on the explicit not-provisioned states. The composition-home question posed here was RESOLVED: the honest option was **(a) home them in `@twt/channels`** — but as a NON-frozen ADDITION (a new `composition/provider-composition.ts` module + barrel exports), NOT a change to any frozen primitive (`dispatch`/`cascade`/`provider`/`render`/`audit`/`CANONICAL_CHANNEL_LADDER`/`DEFAULT_PROVIDER_REGISTRY` untouched). `@twt/channels` already depends on `@twt/domain`, so it is the lowest shared layer that satisfies the composition's deps (it calls the channels provider factories + reads domain's channelConfig); both apps/api and apps/jobs already depend on it ⇒ ZERO new package, ZERO new edge, and apps/api keeps working via re-exports. Option (b) (a second by-value composition in apps/jobs) was therefore NOT needed. [apps/jobs/src/boot.ts; apps/jobs/src/scheduler/contribution-providers.ts; packages/channels/src/composition/provider-composition.ts]

## Deferred from: implementation of 8-10-out-of-band-contribution-policy (2026-07-24)

_Both entries below are **deferred with a gate**, not closed. Neither is an artifact that was produced;
neither is a gap that was silently accepted. Each names the story that must pick it up
([[feedback_closure_language_precision]] — "Closed by [edit]" ≠ "Resolved via explicit deferral")._

- **The member-profile PRIVATE NOTE capability is UN-BUILT — and is deliberately NOT scripted as if it worked.** The UX spec's Madad resolution path (`ux-design-specification.md:1056`, step (c)) has the Helpline Operator "offer to add a privately-visible note to the member's own profile recording the out-of-band gift, for the member's own record-keeping." Story 8.10's Task-0 recon searched `apps/` + `packages/` for any such surface (`private note` / `member note` / `profile note` / `privateNote` / `memberNote` / `private_note`) and found **none** — no table, no column, no endpoint, no screen; the only hits were `deriveMemberNoteRef` in the Contribution Note PDF (a member reference MARK on a generated document, an unrelated concept). Per D4 + [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]], the step therefore ships as a `[PENDING CAPABILITY — Epic 10]` line in `docs/policies/out-of-band-contributions.md` §5 rather than as a live operator-voiced i18n key: an operator must not be scripted to promise a member a feature that does not exist. Consequence to be honest about: **the UX-DR76 four-step resolution path is three steps in practice today** (acknowledge / clarify / do-not-contact-the-family). **Re-trigger:** **Epic 10** (helpdesk console) — the story that builds the operator surface must either build the private-note capability and add the fourth `out_of_band.helpline.*` key pair (en + hi), or explicitly re-defer it and say so. Note the shape constraint if it IS built: a private own-record note is for the MEMBER'S own record-keeping and must not become a tracking/reconciliation path for the gift, which would be unsafe operation (a) — see the §4(a) fence, which will fail on an `out_of_band`-shaped identifier. [docs/policies/out-of-band-contributions.md §5; packages/i18n/locales/{en,hi}/contribution.json]

- **The SAHYOG VIVRAN / analytics NO-CREDIT constraint is declared, not wired.** `epics.md:3037` commits that the trust "never claims credit for [out-of-band gifts] in Sahyog Vivran or analytics", and `epics.md:3038` binds Sahyog Vivran framing to the same no-blame posture as the tutorial and helpline copy. **Epic 11b Story 11b.3 (Sahyog Vivran rendering) is unbuilt**, so Story 8.10 registered the governed surface (`docs/policies/out-of-band-contributions.md` §6) and recorded the constraint, and wrote **no renderer code** (D5 — the Story 8.9 declared-seam convention). The structural half is already enforced today and is not waiting on 11b.3: the §4(a) no-ingest-path fence means there is no out-of-band datum for a renderer to surface even by accident. What is NOT enforced is the **narrative** half — a renderer could still author prose that claims credit for the cadre's direct giving, and no gate catches that. **Re-trigger:** **Epic 11b Story 11b.3** — its copy must pass the `out-of-band-blame` tone rule (add the Sahyog Vivran locale namespace to `microcopy.yaml` `scope.copy_globs` when it lands, per the 7.8 / 8.2 / 8.10 config-only pattern) and must carry the tone-review Publish-routing row for its own surface. Same constraint binds Story 8.12's loop instrumentation: out-of-band gifts are not a metric, a funnel step, or a denominator. [docs/policies/out-of-band-contributions.md §6; microcopy.yaml]

## Deferred from: code review of 9-4-utr-matching-engine-matcher-mechanism (2026-07-26)

_Five entries below are **deferred with a gate**, not closed — real gaps surfaced by the 3-layer adversarial review, none blocking (the Acceptance Auditor independently verified D1/D2/D4/D5/D6, the units reconciliation, and the AC7 8.10 fence all hold)._

- **Sweep re-enqueue (`runReconciliationMatchSweep`) has no per-tenant fairness** — orders strictly by `created_at ASC` across all pariwars, so one tenant with many long-lived live alerts can dominate every batch once `DEFAULT_MATCHER_SWEEP_LIMIT` is hit; the code only alarms on the cap, it never rebalances or rotates. Not exploitable at current scale. **Re-trigger:** if live-alert volume approaches the sweep's batch cap, or the pool/pariwar count grows materially. [apps/jobs/src/matcher/matcher-worker.ts]
- **Keyed-store claim TTL (300s) spans multiple sequential DB round-trips per verdict** (the monotonic pre-read, the append with up to 8 version-conflict retries, `recordResult`, and the best-effort notify enqueue all happen serially inside one claim window). Under DB contention or a retry storm this can plausibly exceed the TTL, letting a concurrent tick reclaim the same `(member, alert, entry)` key. Acknowledged in Dev Notes as a documented "size with headroom" gotcha, same class as the existing `keyed-store` precedent. **Re-trigger:** if a live-DB flake or duplicate-confirmation incident is ever traced to a claim-TTL race. [apps/jobs/src/matcher/matcher-worker.ts]
- **`listPoolStatementUploads`/`listAlertAttestations` load every matching row per tick with no `.limit()`**, unlike the sweep's explicit `sweepLimit` — inconsistent with the "no silent cap" discipline the story applies everywhere else (sweep batch, claim TTL, retry budget). Pool/alert sizes are naturally small today. **Re-trigger:** if a pool accumulates an unusually large number of re-uploaded statements or an alert an unusually large attestation count. [packages/domain/src/reconciliation/matcher-reads.ts]
- **All verdicts for a cycle serialize onto one shared alert stream with one optimistic-concurrency version counter** — a direct consequence of LOCKED Decision D2 (co-locating `contribution.confirmed`/`reconciliation-mismatch` with the `utr-attested` claim they resolve). The worker's sequential per-member confirm/mismatch loop competes for the same version on every tick; as the number of simultaneously-resolving members in one alert grows this degrades into O(n) sequential SAVEPOINT retries. Not this diff's to unilaterally change — D2 is locked. **Re-trigger:** if a large-pool cycle's matcher run is ever observed taking materially longer than the FR-30 p95 < 4h budget, revisit whether D2's stream co-location needs to change. [apps/jobs/src/matcher/matcher-worker.ts]
- **The pg-boss job loop (`for (const job of jobs)`) has no per-job isolation**, so one cycle's failure blocks other cycles queued in the same batch — mirrors an existing precedent already in `cycle-open-alert.ts`, not a new pattern introduced by this diff. **Re-trigger:** whichever story next hardens pg-boss batch-loop isolation generally — not 9.4-specific. [apps/jobs/src/matcher/matcher-worker.ts]

## Deferred from: code review of 9-10-4-hour-retry-reminders (2026-07-28)

- **Pending-match sweep 500-alert cap does not advance across ticks** (`apps/jobs/src/scheduler/contribution-notify-triggers.ts:~356-446`). The reconciling-alert scan is `SELECT ... FROM alerts WHERE current_state IN ('live','closed') ORDER BY a.cycle_id ASC LIMIT $1` with no keyset/offset — inherited verbatim from `runDeadlineReminderSweep` (same ordering, same `>= limit` alarm, same "the next tick will pick them up" text). Because the pending-match sweep additionally scans `closed` alerts (which accumulate across the ~20-day reconciliation tail, unlike the deadline sweep's `live`-only), the 500 bound is materially more reachable: once reconciling alerts exceed 500, the highest-`cycle_id` alerts starve permanently until the count drops, and the alarm's "next tick" reassurance is false for that overflow set. Pre-existing pattern shared by all three contribution-notify sweeps; the proper fix is a codebase-wide keyset cursor, not a 9.10-local change.

## Deferred from: implementation of 9-11-over-payment-facilitated-recovery (2026-07-28)

_Both entries below are **deferred with a gate**, not closed — neither is an artifact that was produced;
each names the story that must pick it up ([[feedback_closure_language_precision]] — "Resolved via explicit
deferral" ≠ "Not addressed"). Both were LOCKED by BigDev before authoring (Decision 2 + the Task-5/6 note)._

- **FR-36's "auto-drafted polite note from the trust to the nominee asking for return of the excess" is UN-BUILT — and is deliberately NOT wired.** FR-36 (`prds/prd-TWT-2026-05-22/prd.md:639-641`, `[v1-S]`) says the trust "auto-drafts a polite note from the trust to the nominee asking for return of the excess." Story 9.11 ships **no** nominee-facing note — no draft, no store, no send, no template, no channel — for two hard blockers (Decision 2): (a) **no delivery channel** — a nominee is a claim contact, not an app member with a Story 5.1 dispatcher identity; the whole notification substrate targets members ([[project_channels_no_live_dispatch_yet]]); (b) **no legal lever** — the money is legally the nominee's the instant it lands (`prds/prd-TWT-2026-05-22/review-adversarial.md:39`), so the note is a *request*, not an instruction; the posture is *facilitated, never enforced* (§4.14). Consequence to be honest about: **the over-payment recovery today is entirely off-band** — a human trustee/helpdesk operator decides and records one of the three new `recover` codes (`refund_difference`/`applied_next_cycle_credit`/`left_as_donation`), and any actual outreach to the nominee happens through whatever channel the nominee really uses (phone, WhatsApp, the claim contact), un-instrumented. **Re-trigger:** **Epic 10** (helpdesk console) — the story that builds the operator surface must let a human draft + send a polite recovery message off-band, or explicitly re-defer and say so. Do NOT invent a nominee-notify producer or a nominee channel to satisfy the epic prose literally (a `packages/channels/src/**`-shaped change is out of scope + breaches the facilitated-never-enforced posture). [prds/prd-TWT-2026-05-22/prd.md:639-641; packages/channels/src/**]

- **The review-queue `in_recovery` flag is hardcoded `false`, so a `recover`-resolved over-payment case never leaves the open queue — SHARPENED by Story 9.11's three terminal codes.** `apps/api/src/modules/reconciliation-review/handlers.ts` returns `in_recovery: false` for every row/detail (a Story 9.8 tracked deferral, D7 — `recover` emits no outcome event to flip it; the v1 audit sink is not queryable). Under Story 9.8 alone this was benign: its only `recover` codes (`member_contacted`/`awaiting_correction`) describe **ongoing** work, so a case staying visibly "open" was correct. Story 9.11 adds three **terminal** codes (`refund_difference`/`applied_next_cycle_credit`/`left_as_donation`) — once a trustee records one, the case has no mechanism to distinguish "resolved, done" from "never touched," and keeps resurfacing in the open queue indistinguishable from an untouched case. **Not fixed in 9.11** — it needs a queue-model change (a durable recovery-resolution marker the `listOpenReconciliationCases` dedup can read) beyond this story's classification/governance scope. **Re-trigger:** whoever revisits Story 9.8's D7 design — **Epic 10** (the helpdesk console) is the natural owner — must give `recover` a durable resolution signal so a terminally-resolved over-payment drops from the open queue. [apps/api/src/modules/reconciliation-review/handlers.ts; packages/domain/src/reconciliation/reconciliation-review-read.ts]

Flagged independently by two review layers. **Re-trigger:** if a future change to this fence, or a near-miss where a forked comparison almost shipped, makes the regex's blind spots worth closing with an AST-based scan. [packages/domain/tests/pool/over-under-single-source.test.ts]
- **Postgres `::int` (int4) casts on the JSONB-extracted `depositedAmountPaise`/`expectedAmountPaise` fields, across three read-paths, would throw a DB error rather than degrade if a deposit ever exceeded ~₹2.15 crore** (int4's ~2.1 billion paise ceiling). Given this system's fixed contribution amounts are modest (hundreds/thousands of INR), the realistic probability is extremely low, but the defect class is real — an oversized amount would crash the read instead of failing soft. **Re-trigger:** if fixed contribution amounts are ever allowed to scale into the crore range, or if this cast pattern is copied into a context with less bounded inputs. Nothing is enforced-missing here — the confirmed-only invariant the public surface needs is already structural in the presenter; what is deferred is only the public read + the web render layer that resolves the emitted keys/token-roles. **Re-trigger:** **Epic 11b** (Sahyog Vivran / Sahyog Drive) — build the public read that feeds `derivePoolProgressCardViewModel` and the web render adapter (RN-parity: `formatInr` + Latin numerals at the boundary, `status-confirmed` token → the web palette). - **Two concurrent `createRoutingPolicyVersion` calls racing on the same `(pariwar_id, version)` is untested.** The 23505-to-`RoutingPolicyVersionConflictError` mapping added in chunk 1 is exercised nowhere — a genuine two-connections-racing test is higher-effort and more flake-prone in a shared CI environment than the sequential tests this suite otherwise uses. **Re-trigger:** a dedicated concurrency-hardening test pass for the registry write path. [packages/domain/tests/integration/helpdesk/registry.spec.ts]
- **Two concurrent `projectTicketGenesis` calls racing on the same `ticket_id` is untested.** Only the sequential `HelpdeskGenesisAlreadyExistsError` pre-check path is exercised; the real `(stream_id, event_version)` unique-violation → `HelpdeskStreamConcurrencyError` path (chunk 1) has never actually fired in a test. Same concurrency-test-cost reasoning as above. **Re-trigger:** same concurrency-hardening pass, extended to the projector. [packages/domain/tests/integration/helpdesk/projector-trigger.spec.ts]
- **No end-to-end wiring test threads a real routing decision + SLA calculation through the projector.** `projector-trigger.spec.ts`'s `genesisInput` fixture hardcodes `assignedAt`/`slaFirstResponseDue`/`slaResolutionDue` as static literals instead of deriving them via `resolveRoute` (against a real policy document) + `computeTicketSlaDueDates`/`businessDaysDeadline` — so routing.ts, registry.ts, and project.ts are each unit-tested in isolation but never proven wired together correctly. **Re-trigger:** a dedicated end-to-end integration test (or Story 10.2/10.3's own integration coverage of the full create-ticket flow, which exercises this wiring for real via the API route). [packages/domain/tests/integration/helpdesk/projector-trigger.spec.ts]

## Deferred from: code review of 10-1-helpdesk-subsystem-data-model-routing-policy-registry, chunk 3/6 (2026-07-28)

_One entry below is **deferred**, not closed — surfaced reviewing chunk 3/6 ("contracts")._

- **`HelpdeskAttachment` has no size field, no `content_type` allowlist, and no `filename` path-traversal/control-character sanitization.** Per the story's own Dev Notes scope boundary, Story 10.1 defines only the object-store REFERENCE shape (attachments modeled as references, never bytes); the actual upload transport — where size limits, MIME allowlisting, and filename sanitization become load-bearing (a real uploaded file, a real storage write) — is Story 10.2 (member ticket-filing) / 10.4 (admin console) to build. **Re-trigger:** whichever of 10.2/10.4 builds the actual attachment upload handler must add these validations at that point, not defer them further. [packages/contracts/src/helpdesk/ticket.ts]

## Deferred from: code review of 10-1-helpdesk-subsystem-data-model-routing-policy-registry, chunk 4/6 (2026-07-28)

_Four entries below are **deferred with a gate**, not closed — surfaced reviewing chunk 4/6 ("API route"). Two of these were already informal notes in the story's own Dev Agent Record; this converts them into a properly tracked, re-triggerable commitment rather than leaving them as prose only ([[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]] — un-gated action items decay)._

- **No `requirePermissionHook` on the create-ticket route — any actor holding any role grant in a Pariwar, regardless of role, can create and route helpdesk tickets today.** Every comparable sibling admin-write route (`pool-fixed-amount`, `channel-config`, `degraded-mode`, `reconciliation-review`, `wa-opt-in`) chains a specific `requirePermissionHook(deps, '<permission.key>', ...)` after `[adminSession, scope]`; `apps/api/src/modules/helpdesk/routes.ts` only has `[adminSession, scope]`. Already an explicit Dev Agent Record note ("a dedicated `helpdesk.create` permission... land[s] with Story 10.2") — not silently missed, but adding the permission now requires RBAC-catalog + role-bundle-seeding changes (`packages/domain/src/rbac/roles.ts`) that are a bigger cross-cutting decision than this route file alone. **Re-trigger:** Story 10.2/10.3, whichever lands the operator/member-facing surface first, must add a `helpdesk.create` (or similarly-scoped) permission key to the RBAC catalog, seed it onto the appropriate role bundles, and wire `requirePermissionHook` into this route (and any new ones). [apps/api/src/modules/helpdesk/routes.ts]
- **No idempotency protection on ticket creation** — a client retry (timeout, double-submit) mints a fresh `ticket_id` (a plain random UUID, no natural key to derive from) and creates a duplicate ticket. Already an explicit Dev Notes decision ("If a helpline-call create ever needs idempotency, it uses a keyed idempotency store, NOT a derived id"). **Re-trigger:** whichever of Story 10.2 (member app, subject to real client retry behavior) or 10.3 (helpline operator UI) first needs retry-safety should add a keyed idempotency store keyed on a client-supplied idempotency token, mirroring the pattern other primitives in this codebase already use for retry-prone writes. [apps/api/src/modules/helpdesk/handlers.ts]
- **No existence/cross-tenant validation for `subject_member_id`/`subject_actor_id` before persisting a ticket** — a caller can reference a nonexistent or cross-tenant member/actor id with no error; the column is unFK'd (no DB-level foreign key). Deferred rather than patched ad-hoc in this route because it looks like a broader systemic pattern (unFK'd subject/reference columns trusting the caller/session context) across other primitives in this codebase, not a helpdesk-specific oversight. **Re-trigger:** a dedicated cross-primitive investigation into whether/where subject-reference existence validation is expected at all in this codebase's primitives, then apply consistently — not a one-off fix here. [apps/api/src/modules/helpdesk/handlers.ts]
- **No existence/cross-tenant validation for the cross-link seam refs (`claim_case_id`/`pool_id`/`module_id`/`validity_lookup_id`)** — matches the story's own explicit scope boundary (Dev Notes: "nullable seams... the navigation that reads them is Story 10.4"), so this is a re-confirmation of an already-stated boundary, not a new gap. **Re-trigger:** Story 10.4, when it builds the navigation that actually reads these refs, should validate they resolve to real, same-tenant records at that point. [apps/api/src/modules/helpdesk/handlers.ts]
